&-\underbrace{2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}((u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma)\cdot\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}(c_{s}^{-2}\partial_{\alpha}\partial_{\lambda}h\partial^{\alpha}w^{\lambda})dx}_{\equiv\mathrm{H}_{12c}}.\end{split}

(3.52)

We now discuss
\mathrm{H}_{12a},\mathrm{H}_{12b}
, and
\mathrm{H}_{12c}
as follows. Note (
2.29
) and (
1.18
). By Hölder’s inequality, (
3.51
), and Lemma
2.5
, it yields

\begin{split}|\mathrm{H}_{12a}|\lesssim&\|\Gamma\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}\|\Psi\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{\dot{B}_{\infty,2}^{s_{0}-2}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}})(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}})\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}\\
&+(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}})(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{3}_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{4}_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}).\end{split}

(3.53)

For
\mathrm{H}_{12b}
, we cannot bound it directly like
\mathrm{H}_{12a}
. We decompose
\mathrm{H}_{12b}
by

\begin{split}\mathrm{H}_{12b}=&-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}((u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma)\cdot\left\{\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}(u^{0}\partial^{\gamma}w^{\lambda}\partial_{t}\partial_{\gamma}u_{\lambda})+\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}(u^{i}\partial^{\gamma}w^{\lambda}\partial_{i}\partial_{\gamma}u_{\lambda})\right\}dx\\
=&\underbrace{-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}((u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma)\cdot\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}(u^{0}\partial^{0}w^{\lambda}\partial^{2}_{t}u_{\lambda})dx}_{\equiv\mathrm{H}_{12b1}}\underbrace{-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}((u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma)\cdot\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}(u^{0}\partial^{i}w^{\lambda}\partial_{t}\partial_{i}u_{\lambda})dx}_{\equiv\mathrm{H}_{12b2}}\\
&\underbrace{-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}((u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma)\cdot\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}(u^{i}\partial^{\gamma}w^{\lambda}\partial_{i}\partial_{\gamma}u_{\lambda})dx}_{\equiv\mathrm{H}_{12b3}}.\end{split}

(3.54)

Due to (
1.20
), we have

\displaystyle\partial^{2}_{t}u_{\kappa}=

\displaystyle g^{0i}\partial^{2}_{ti}u_{\kappa}+g^{ij}\partial^{2}_{ij}u_{\kappa}+c_{s}^{2}\Omega\mathrm{e}^{-h}W_{\kappa}-Q_{\kappa},

(3.55)

\displaystyle\partial^{2}_{t}h=

\displaystyle g^{0i}\partial^{2}_{ti}h+g^{ij}\partial^{2}_{ij}h-D.

(3.56)

Inserting (
3.55
) to
\mathrm{H}_{12b1}
, we derive that

\begin{split}&\mathrm{H}_{12b1}\\
=&\underbrace{-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}((u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma)\cdot\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}(u_{0}\partial_{t}w^{\lambda}g^{0i}\partial^{2}_{ti}u_{\lambda})dx}_{\equiv\mathrm{H}_{12ba}}\underbrace{-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}((u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma)\cdot\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}(u_{0}\partial_{t}w^{\lambda}g^{ij}\partial^{2}_{ij}u_{\lambda})dx}_{\equiv\mathrm{H}_{12bb}}\\
&\underbrace{+2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}((u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma)\cdot\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}(u_{0}\partial_{t}w^{\lambda}Q_{\lambda})dx}_{\equiv\mathrm{H}_{12bc}}\underbrace{-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}((u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma)\cdot\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}(u_{0}\partial_{t}w^{\lambda}c_{s}^{2}\Omega\mathrm{e}^{-h}W_{\lambda})dx}_{\equiv\mathrm{H}_{12bd}}.\end{split}

(3.57)

For
\mathrm{H}_{12ba}
, we can compute out

\begin{split}&\mathrm{H}_{12ba}\\
=&-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}((u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma)\cdot\partial_{i}\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}(u_{0}\partial_{t}w^{\lambda}g^{0i}\partial_{t}u_{\lambda})dx+2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}((u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma)\cdot\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}(\partial_{i}u_{0}\partial_{t}w^{\lambda}g^{0i}\partial_{t}u_{\lambda})dx\\
&+2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}((u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma)\cdot\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}(u_{0}\partial_{i}\partial_{t}w^{\lambda}g^{0i}\partial_{t}u_{\lambda})dx+2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}((u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma)\cdot\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}(u_{0}\partial_{t}w^{\lambda}\partial_{i}g^{0i}\partial_{t}u_{\lambda})dx.\end{split}

(3.58)

For the first term in the right hand side of (
3.58
), we can bound it by Plancherel’s formula and Hölder’s inequality. For the rest terms, we can estimate them by using Hölder’s inequality and Lemma
2.5
. Therefore, it follows

\begin{split}|\mathrm{H}_{12ba}|\lesssim&\|\Gamma\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-\frac{3}{2}}}\|d\mathbf{w}d\mathbf{u}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-\frac{3}{2}}}+\|\Gamma\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-2}}\|d\mathbf{u}\nabla d\mathbf{w}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-2}}\\
&+\|\Gamma\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-2}}\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\cdot d\mathbf{w}\cdot(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-2}}\\
\lesssim&(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{\dot{B}_{\infty,2}^{s_{0}-2}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}})(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}})\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}\\
&+(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}})(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{4}_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}).\end{split}

(3.59)

Similarly, we can calculate
\mathrm{H}_{12bb}
by

\begin{split}\mathrm{H}_{12bb}=&-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}((u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma)\cdot\partial_{i}\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}(u_{0}\partial_{t}w^{\lambda}g^{ij}\partial_{j}u_{\lambda})dx+2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}((u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma)\cdot\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}(\partial_{i}u_{0}\partial_{t}w^{\lambda}g^{ij}\partial_{j}u_{\lambda})dx\\
&+2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}((u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma)\cdot\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}(u_{0}\partial_{i}\partial_{t}w^{\lambda}g^{ij}\partial_{j}u_{\lambda})dx+2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}((u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma)\cdot\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}(u_{0}\partial_{i}\partial_{t}w^{\lambda}\partial_{i}g^{ij}\partial_{j}u_{\lambda})dx.\end{split}

Due to Plancherel formula, Hölder’s inequality, and Lemma
2.5
again, we can bound
\mathrm{H}_{12bb}
by

\begin{split}|\mathrm{H}_{12bb}|\lesssim&\|\Gamma\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-\frac{3}{2}}}\|d\mathbf{w}d\mathbf{u}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-\frac{3}{2}}}+\|\Gamma\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-2}}(\|d\mathbf{u}d\mathbf{w}d\mathbf{u}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-2}}+\|d\mathbf{u}\nabla d\mathbf{w}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-2}})\\
\lesssim&(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{\dot{B}_{\infty,2}^{s_{0}-2}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}})(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}})\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}\\
&+(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}})(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{4}_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}).\end{split}

(3.60)

By Hölder’s inequality and Lemma
2.5
, we can estimate
\mathrm{H}_{12bc}
and
\mathrm{H}_{12bd}
by

\begin{split}|\mathrm{H}_{12bc}|\lesssim&\|\Gamma\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-2}}\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\cdot(d\mathbf{u},dh)\cdot d\mathbf{w}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-2}}\\
\lesssim&(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}})(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{3}_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{4}_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}).\end{split}

(3.61)

and

\begin{split}|\mathrm{H}_{12bd}|\lesssim&\|\Gamma\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-2}}(\|d\mathbf{w}d\mathbf{w}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-2}}+\|d\mathbf{u}dhd\mathbf{w}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-2}})\\
\lesssim&(\|d\mathbf{u}\|_{\dot{B}_{\infty,2}^{s_{0}-2}}+\|d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}})\|d\mathbf{w}\|_{H^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}\|d\mathbf{w}\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}\|d\mathbf{w}\|_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}\\
&+(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}})(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{3}_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{4}_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}})\\
\lesssim&(\|d\mathbf{u}\|_{\dot{B}_{\infty,2}^{s_{0}-2}}+\|d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}})(\|d\mathbf{u}\|_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}+\|d\mathbf{u}\|_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}\|dh\|_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}})\|d\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}\\
&+(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}})(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{4}_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}).\end{split}

(3.62)

To summarize (
3.57
), (
3.59
), (
3.60
), (
3.61
), and (
3.62
), we get

\begin{split}|\mathrm{H}_{12b1}|\lesssim&(\|d\mathbf{u}\|_{\dot{B}_{\infty,2}^{s_{0}-2}}+\|d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}})(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}})\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}\\
&+(\|d\mathbf{u}\|_{\dot{B}_{\infty,2}^{s_{0}-2}}+\|d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}})(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}})\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}\\
&+(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}})(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{4}_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}).\end{split}

(3.63)

By the chain rule, we are able to obtain

\begin{split}&\mathrm{H}_{12b2}\\
=&-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}((u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma)\cdot\partial_{i}\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}(u^{0}\partial^{i}w^{\lambda}\partial_{t}u_{\lambda})dx+2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}((u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma)\cdot\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}(\partial_{i}u^{0}\partial^{i}w^{\lambda}\partial_{t}u_{\lambda})dx\\
&+2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}((u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma)\cdot\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}(u^{0}\partial_{i}\partial^{i}w^{\lambda}\partial_{t}u_{\lambda})dx,\end{split}

and

\begin{split}&\mathrm{H}_{12b3}\\
=&-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}((u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma)\cdot\partial_{i}\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}(u^{i}\partial^{\gamma}w^{\lambda}\partial_{\gamma}u_{\lambda})dx+2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}((u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma)\cdot\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}(\partial_{i}u^{i}\partial^{\gamma}w^{\lambda}\partial_{\gamma}u_{\lambda})dx\\
&+2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}((u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma)\cdot\Lambda_{x}^{s_{0}-2}(u^{i}\partial_{i}\partial^{\gamma}w^{\lambda}\partial_{\gamma}u_{\lambda})dx.\end{split}

Therefore, using the Plancherel formula, Hölder’s inequality and Lemma
2.5
again, we obtain

\begin{split}&|\mathrm{H}_{12b2}|+|\mathrm{H}_{12b3}|\\
\lesssim&\|\Gamma\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-\frac{3}{2}}}\|d\mathbf{w}d\mathbf{u}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-\frac{3}{2}}}+\|\Gamma\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-2}}(\|d\mathbf{u}d\mathbf{w}d\mathbf{u}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-2}}+\|d\mathbf{u}\nabla d\mathbf{w}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-2}})\\
\lesssim&(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{\dot{B}_{\infty,2}^{s_{0}-2}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}})(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}})\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}\\
&+(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}})(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{4}_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}).\end{split}

(3.64)

Summarizing our outcome (
3.54
), (
3.63
), and (
3.64
), so it yields

\begin{split}|\mathrm{H}_{12b}|\lesssim&(\|d\mathbf{u}\|_{\dot{B}_{\infty,2}^{s_{0}-2}}+\|d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}})(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}})\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}\\
&+(\|d\mathbf{u}\|_{\dot{B}_{\infty,2}^{s_{0}-2}}+\|d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}})(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}})\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}\\
&+(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}})(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{4}_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}).\end{split}

(3.65)

We still need to bound
\mathrm{H}_{12c}
. A direct calculation tells us

&+{\int^{t}_{0}}(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{\dot{B}_{\infty,2}^{s_{0}-2}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}})(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}})\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{3}_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}d\tau\\
&+{\int^{t}_{0}}(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{\dot{B}_{\infty,2}^{s_{0}-2}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}})(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}})\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{4}_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}d\tau\\
&+{\int^{t}_{0}}(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}})(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{4}_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}})d\tau.\end{split}

(3.81)

Taking
a=s
in (
3.1
), (
3.3
), and (
3.4
), using (
3.81
), we have

\begin{split}E_{s}(t)\lesssim&E_{s}(0)+(E_{s}(0)^{\frac{3}{2}}+E_{s}(0)^{2}+E_{s}(0)^{5})\exp(5\int^{t}_{0}\|d\mathbf{u},dh\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}d\tau)\\
&+E_{s}(0)^{3}+{\int^{t}_{0}}(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{\dot{B}_{\infty,2}^{s_{0}-2}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}})E_{s}(\tau)d\tau\\
&+{\int^{t}_{0}}(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{\dot{B}_{\infty,2}^{s_{0}-2}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}})E_{s}(0)\exp(\int^{\tau}_{0}\|d\mathbf{u},dh\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}dz)E_{s}(\tau)d\tau\\
&+{\int^{t}_{0}}(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{\dot{B}_{\infty,2}^{s_{0}-2}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}})E_{s}(0)^{\frac{3}{2}}\exp(\frac{3}{2}\int^{\tau}_{0}\|d\mathbf{u},dh\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}dz)E_{s}(\tau)d\tau\\
&+{\int^{t}_{0}}(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{\dot{B}_{\infty,2}^{s_{0}-2}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}})E_{s}(0)^{2}\exp(2\int^{\tau}_{0}\|d\mathbf{u},dh\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}dz)E_{s}(\tau)d\tau\\
&+{\int^{t}_{0}}E_{s}(\tau)\left\{E_{s}(0)\exp(\int^{\tau}_{0}\|d\mathbf{u},dh\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}dz)+E_{s}(0)^{2}\exp(2\int^{\tau}_{0}\|d\mathbf{u},dh\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}dz)\right\}d\tau.\end{split}

Observing
E_{s}(0)^{\frac{3}{2}},E_{s}(0)^{2},E_{s}(0)^{2}\leq\max\{E_{s}(0),E_{s}(0)^{5}\}
, so we have

\begin{split}E_{s}(t)\lesssim&E_{s}(0)+E_{s}(0)^{5}+(E_{s}(0)+E_{s}(0)^{5})\exp(5\int^{t}_{0}\|d\mathbf{u},dh\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}d\tau)\\
&+{\int^{t}_{0}}(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{\dot{B}_{\infty,2}^{s_{0}-2}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}})E_{s}(\tau)d\tau\\
&+{\int^{t}_{0}}(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{\dot{B}_{\infty,2}^{s_{0}-2}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}})E_{s}(0)\exp(\int^{t}_{0}\|d\mathbf{u},dh\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}dz)E_{s}(\tau)d\tau\\
&+{\int^{t}_{0}}(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{\dot{B}_{\infty,2}^{s_{0}-2}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}})E_{s}(0)^{2}\exp(2\int^{t}_{0}\|d\mathbf{u},dh\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}dz)E_{s}(\tau)d\tau\\
&+{\int^{t}_{0}}E_{s}(\tau)\left\{E_{s}(0)\exp(\int^{t}_{0}\|d\mathbf{u},dh\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}dz)+E_{s}(0)^{2}\exp(2\int^{t}_{0}\|d\mathbf{u},dh\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}dz)\right\}d\tau.\end{split}

Therefore, (
3.5
)-(
3.6
) can be immediately obtained after an application of Gronwall’s inequality.
∎

With a relaxed regularity assumption on the vorticity, we can obtain another type of energy estimates.

3.2.
Energy estimate for Theorem
1.3

Theorem 3.3

(Energy estimate: type 2)
.

Let
(h,\mathbf{u})
be a solution of (
1.13
). Let
\mathbf{w}
be defined in (
1.15
). For
2<s\leq\frac{5}{2}
, the following energy estimate hold:

\|\tilde{E}_{s}(t)\|_{H^{s}_{x}}\leq\tilde{E}_{0}\exp\left\{5{\int^{t}_{0}}\|(dh,d\mathbf{u})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}d\tau\cdot\exp\big(5{\int^{t}_{0}}\|(dh,d\mathbf{u})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}d\tau\big)\right\},

(3.82)

where

\begin{split}\tilde{E}_{s}(t)=&\|h\|^{2}_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|u^{0}-1\|^{2}_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|\mathring{\mathbf{u}}\|^{2}_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H_{x}^{2}},\\
\tilde{E}_{0}=&C\left(\|(h_{0},\mathring{\mathbf{u}}_{0})\|^{2}_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|^{2}_{H^{2}}+\|(h_{0},\mathring{\mathbf{u}}_{0})\|^{10}_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|^{10}_{H^{2}}\right).\end{split}

(3.83)

Above,
C
is an universal constant.

Proof.

For we have proved (
3.1
) and (
3.7
), so we only need to bound
\|\mathbf{w}\|_{{H}_{x}^{2}}
. We consider

\|\mathbf{w}\|_{{H}_{x}^{2}}=\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{2}}.

(3.84)

So it’s natural for us to utilize the transport structure of
\mathbf{w}
and the higher derivatives
\mathbf{W}
and
\mathbf{G}
. By (
3.11
), so we have

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{w}(t,\cdot)\|^{2}_{L_{x}^{2}}\lesssim&\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|^{2}_{L_{x}^{2}}+\int^{t}_{0}\|dh,d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{L_{x}^{2}}d\tau.\end{split}

(3.85)

Note that

\|\mathbf{w}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{2}}=\|\Delta\mathbf{w}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}.

(3.86)

Recall
\mathring{\mathbf{w}}=(w^{1},w^{2},w^{3})
. Let us first consider
\|\mathring{\mathbf{w}}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{2}}
. Recall
\Delta_{H}
as in (
3.13
). Then we get

\|\Delta_{H}\mathring{\mathbf{w}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\lesssim\|\Delta\mathring{\mathbf{w}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\lesssim\|\Delta_{H}\mathring{\mathbf{w}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}.

(3.87)

Due to (
2.97
), we have

\|\Delta_{H}\mathring{\mathbf{w}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\lesssim\|\mathbf{u}\cdot\mathrm{vort}\mathbf{w}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{1}}+\|\mathbf{w}\cdot(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{1}}+\|d\mathbf{u}\cdot\nabla\mathring{\mathbf{w}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}.

(3.88)

Using (
1.18
), (
2.99
), and Lemma
2.4
, it follows

\begin{split}\|\Delta_{H}\mathring{\mathbf{w}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\lesssim&\|\mathbf{u}\cdot\mathbf{W}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{1}}+\|\mathbf{w}\cdot(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{1}}+\|d\mathbf{u}\cdot\nabla\mathring{\mathbf{w}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\\
\lesssim&\|\mathbf{W}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{1}}+\|\mathring{\mathbf{w}}\cdot(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{1}}+\|d\mathbf{u}\cdot\nabla\mathring{\mathbf{w}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\\
\lesssim&\|\mathbf{W}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{1}}+\|\mathring{\mathbf{w}}\|_{{H}_{x}^{\frac{3}{2}}}\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{{H}_{x}^{s-1}}.\end{split}

(3.89)

Combining (
3.87
), (
3.88
) and (
3.89
), we obtain

\begin{split}\|\Delta\mathring{\mathbf{w}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\lesssim&\|\mathbf{W}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{1}}+\|\mathring{\mathbf{w}}\|_{{H}_{x}^{\frac{3}{2}}}\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{{H}_{x}^{s-1}}.\end{split}

(3.90)

Seeing from (
2.99
), we can obtain

\begin{split}\|\Delta{w^{0}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\lesssim&\|\Delta\mathring{\mathbf{w}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}+\|\Delta\mathbf{u}\cdot\mathring{\mathbf{w}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}+\|\nabla\mathring{\mathbf{w}}\cdot\nabla\mathbf{u}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}.\end{split}

(3.91)

By Hölder’s inequality, then (
3.91
) becomes

\begin{split}\|\Delta{w^{0}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\lesssim&\|\Delta\mathring{\mathbf{w}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}+\|\mathring{\mathbf{w}}\|_{{H}_{x}^{\frac{3}{2}}}\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{{H}_{x}^{s-1}}.\end{split}

(3.92)

Inserting (
3.90
) to (
3.92
), we then derive that

\begin{split}\|\Delta\mathbf{w}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\lesssim&\|\mathbf{W}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{1}}+\|\mathring{\mathbf{w}}\|_{{H}_{x}^{\frac{3}{2}}}\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{{H}_{x}^{s-1}}.\end{split}

(3.93)

By using (
1.18
), (
2.17
), and (
2.99
), we can also prove

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{W}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{1}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{u}\cdot d\mathbf{w}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{1}}+\|\mathbf{w}\cdot dh\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\nabla\mathbf{w}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{1}}+\|\mathring{\mathbf{w}}\cdot(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\Delta\mathbf{w}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}+\|\mathring{\mathbf{w}}\|_{{H}_{x}^{\frac{3}{2}}}\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{{H}_{x}^{s-1}}.

(3.94)

From (
3.93
) and (
3.2
), there is only a lower order term between
\|\mathbf{W}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{1}}
and
\|\Delta\mathbf{w}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}
. So we bound
\|\mathbf{W}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{1}}
as follows.

Step 1:
\|\mathbf{W}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{1}}
. We also first consider
\|\mathring{\mathbf{W}}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{1}}
. On one hand, we have

\|\mathring{\mathbf{W}}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{1}}=\|\nabla\mathring{\mathbf{W}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}.

(3.95)

By (
2.105
), (
3.95
) and (
1.18
), we therefore get

\begin{split}\|\mathring{\mathbf{W}}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{1}}\lesssim&\|\mathbf{G}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{W}\cdot(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|d\mathbf{w}\cdot(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
&+\|\mathbf{w}\cdot d\mathbf{u}\cdot dh\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\cdot\mathbf{w}\cdot dh\|_{L^{2}_{x}}.\end{split}

(3.96)

Using (
1.18
), (
2.100
), (
2.101
), (
1.15
) and Hölder’s inequality, then (
3.96
) becomes

\begin{split}\|\mathring{\mathbf{W}}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{1}}\lesssim&\|\mathbf{G}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|d\mathbf{w}\cdot(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\cdot d\mathbf{u}\cdot dh\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\cdot\mathbf{w}\cdot dh\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\|\mathbf{G}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\nabla\mathbf{w}\cdot(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\cdot d\mathbf{u}\cdot dh\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\|\mathbf{G}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|_{{H}_{x}^{\frac{3}{2}}}\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{{H}_{x}^{s-1}}(1+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{{H}_{x}^{s-1}}).\end{split}

(3.97)

Since (
2.109
), we infer

\begin{split}\|{W}^{0}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{1}}\lesssim&\|\mathring{\mathbf{W}}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{1}}.\end{split}

(3.98)

Adding (
3.97
) and (
3.98
), we have

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{W}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{1}}\lesssim&\|\mathbf{G}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|_{{H}_{x}^{\frac{3}{2}}}\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{{H}_{x}^{s-1}}(1+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{{H}_{x}^{s-1}}).\end{split}

(3.99)

Combining (
3.93
) with (
3.99
), it yields

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{w}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{1}}\leq&C\|\mathbf{G}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+C\|\mathbf{w}\|_{{H}_{x}^{\frac{3}{2}}}\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{{H}_{x}^{s-1}}(1+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{{H}_{x}^{s-1}}).\end{split}

(3.100)

By interpolation formula and Young’s inequality, we get

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{w}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s-1}}\leq&C\|\mathbf{G}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+C\|\mathbf{w}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{{H}_{x}^{1}}\|\mathbf{w}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{{H}_{x}^{2}}\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{{H}_{x}^{s-1}}(1+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{{H}_{x}^{s-1}})\\
\leq&C\|\mathbf{G}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\frac{1}{10}\|\mathbf{w}\|_{{H}_{x}^{2}}+C\|\mathbf{w}\|_{{H}_{x}^{s-1}}\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{{H}_{x}^{s-1}}(1+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{{H}_{x}^{s-1}})\\
\leq&C\|\mathbf{G}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\frac{1}{10}\|\mathbf{w}\|_{{H}_{x}^{2}}+C\|d\mathbf{u}\|_{{H}_{x}^{s-1}}\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{{H}_{x}^{s-1}}(1+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{{H}_{x}^{s-1}}).\end{split}

(3.101)

Step 2:
\|\mathbf{G}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}
. By (
3.28
),
multiplying
G_{\alpha}-F_{\alpha}
on (
3.28
) and integrating it on
\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we can derive that

\begin{split}\frac{d}{dt}\|\mathbf{G}-\mathbf{F}\|^{2}_{L_{x}^{2}}=&\mathrm{I}_{0}+\mathrm{I}_{1}+\mathrm{I}_{2}+\mathrm{I}_{3},\end{split}

(3.102)

where

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}_{0}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{i}(\frac{u^{i}}{u^{0}})(G^{\alpha}-F^{\alpha})\cdot(G_{\alpha}-F_{\alpha})dx,

(3.103)

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}_{1}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial^{\alpha}\big((u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma\big)\cdot(G_{\alpha}-F_{\alpha})dx,

(3.104)

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}_{2}={\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}}(u^{0})^{-1}E^{\alpha}\cdot(G_{\alpha}-F_{\alpha})dx,

(3.105)

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}_{3}=-{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}}\Gamma\partial^{\alpha}\left((u^{0})^{-1}\right)\cdot(G_{\alpha}-F_{\alpha})dx.

(3.106)

For
\mathrm{I}_{1}
is the most difficult term, so we postpone to discuss it.

The bound for
\mathrm{I}_{0},\mathrm{I}_{2},\mathrm{I}_{3}
.
To get the bound of
\mathrm{I}_{0},\mathrm{I}_{2},\mathrm{I}_{3}
, using the Hölder’s inequality is enough. Due to Hölder’s inequality, we get

\displaystyle|\mathrm{I}_{0}|\leq

\displaystyle\|d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}(\|\mathbf{G}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{F}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}).

(3.107)

and

\displaystyle|\mathrm{I}_{2}|\leq

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{E}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}(\|\mathbf{G}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{F}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}).

(3.108)

Recalling (
2.30
), and using Hölder’s inequality, then it follows

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{F}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\cdot d\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\cdot(d\mathbf{u},dh)\cdot\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{w}\|_{{H}^{2}_{x}}\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{{H}^{s-1}_{x}}(1+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{{H}^{s-1}_{x}}).

(3.109)

Seeing from (
2.31
), and using Hölder’s inequality, it yields

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{E}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim&\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\cdot d^{2}\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\cdot(d\mathbf{u},dh)\cdot(d\mathbf{u},dh)\cdot\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
&+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\cdot\mathbf{w}\cdot(d^{2}\mathbf{u},d^{2}h)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\cdot(d\mathbf{u},dh)\cdot d\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}(\|\mathbf{w}\|_{H^{2}_{x}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{H^{s-1}_{x}}\|\mathbf{w}\|_{H^{2}_{x}})\\
&+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}\|\mathbf{w}\|_{H^{2}_{x}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{3}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}\|\mathbf{w}\|_{H^{2}_{x}}.\end{split}

(3.110)

By (
3.38
), so we have

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{G}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim&\|d^{2}\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|(dh,d\mathbf{u})\cdot d\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\cdot(d^{2}\mathbf{u},d^{2}h)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
&+\|(dh,d\mathbf{u})\cdot(dh,d\mathbf{u})\cdot\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\|\mathbf{w}\|_{H^{2}_{x}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{H^{s-1}_{x}}\|\mathbf{w}\|_{H^{2}_{x}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}\|\mathbf{w}\|_{H^{2}_{x}}.\end{split}

(3.111)

By using (
3.2
),(
3.110
), (
3.111
), (
3.108
), and (
3.107
), we have

\begin{split}|\mathrm{I}_{0}|+|\mathrm{I}_{2}|\lesssim&\|d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{x}})(1+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s-1}_{x}})\\
&+\|d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{x}})\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{3}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}\\
&+(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{x}})(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s-1}_{x}})\\
&+(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{x}})(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{3}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{4}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}).\end{split}

(3.112)

For
\mathrm{I}_{3}
, we can show that

\displaystyle|\mathrm{I}_{3}|\leq

\displaystyle\|\Gamma\cdot d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|\mathbf{G}-\mathbf{F}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\lesssim\|d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{x}}(1+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{3}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}).

(3.113)

The bound for
\mathrm{I}_{1}
. Seeing (
3.104
), and noting
\Gamma\approx d\mathbf{u}\cdot d\mathbf{w}
, there is a loss of derivative in
\partial^{\alpha}\Gamma
. So we seek to capture the cancellation of highest derivatives from integrating by parts. To do that, we divide
\mathrm{I}_{1}
by two parts

\begin{split}\mathrm{I}_{1}=&\underbrace{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{\alpha}((u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma)\cdot G^{\alpha}dx}_{\equiv\mathrm{I}_{11}}\underbrace{-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{\alpha}((u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma)\cdot F^{\alpha}dx}_{\equiv\mathrm{I}_{12}}.\end{split}

(3.114)

Integrating by parts on
\mathrm{I}_{11}
and using (
3.44
), so we get

\begin{split}\mathrm{I}_{11}=&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{\alpha}((u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma\cdot G^{\alpha})dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma\cdot\partial_{\alpha}G^{\alpha}dx\\
=&\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma\cdot G^{0}dx\underbrace{-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma\cdot\partial_{\alpha}G^{\alpha}dx}_{\equiv\mathrm{I}_{111}}.\end{split}

(3.115)

Similarly, we calculate
\mathrm{I}_{12}
as

\begin{split}\mathrm{I}_{12}=&-\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma\cdot F^{0}dx+\underbrace{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma\cdot\partial_{\alpha}F^{\alpha}dx}_{\equiv\mathrm{I}_{121}}.\end{split}

(3.116)

Inserting (
3.114
), (
3.115
) and (
3.116
) to (
3.102
), it follows

\begin{split}\frac{d}{dt}\left(\|\mathbf{G}-\mathbf{F}\|^{2}_{L_{x}^{2}}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma\cdot(G^{0}-F^{0})dx\right)=&\mathrm{I}_{0}+\mathrm{I}_{2}+\mathrm{I}_{3}+\mathrm{I}_{111}+\mathrm{I}_{121}.\end{split}

(3.117)

Note (
3.112
) and (
3.113
), so it only remains for us to bound
\mathrm{I}_{111}
and
\mathrm{I}_{121}
.

The bound for
\mathrm{I}_{111}
.
By Lemma
2.5
, Hölder’s inequality, (
2.29
) and (
1.18
), we infer

\begin{split}|\mathrm{I}_{111}|=&|\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma\cdot(\partial_{\alpha}u_{\beta}\partial_{\gamma}W_{\delta})dx|\\
\lesssim&\|\Gamma\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\|d\mathbf{u}\cdot d\mathbf{W}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\\
\lesssim&\|d\mathbf{u}\cdot d\mathbf{w}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}(\|d\mathbf{u}\cdot(d\mathbf{u},dh)\cdot d\mathbf{w}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}+\|d\mathbf{u}\cdot d^{2}\mathbf{w}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}+\|\mathbf{w}\cdot d^{2}h\|_{L_{x}^{2}})\\
\lesssim&\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{x}})\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{H^{s-1}_{x}}\\
&+(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{x}})\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{3}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}.\end{split}

(3.118)

The bound for
\mathrm{I}_{121}
.
Due to (
3.49
), using Hölder’s inequality, we can bound
\Psi
by

\begin{split}\|\Psi\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\lesssim&\|(dh,d\mathbf{u})\cdot d^{2}\mathbf{w}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}+\|(dh,d\mathbf{u})\cdot(dh,d\mathbf{u})\cdot d\mathbf{w}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\\
&+\|(dh,d\mathbf{u})\cdot\mathbf{w}\cdot(d^{2}h,d^{2}\mathbf{u})\|_{L_{x}^{2}}+\|(dh,d\mathbf{u})\cdot\mathbf{w}\cdot(dh,d\mathbf{u})\cdot(dh,d\mathbf{u})\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\\
\lesssim&\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|_{H^{2}_{x}})\\
&+(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|_{H^{2}_{x}})(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{3}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}),\end{split}

(3.119)

Combining (
3.116
) and (
3.49
), we have

\begin{split}\mathrm{I}_{121}=&\underbrace{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma\cdot\Psi dx}_{\equiv\mathrm{I}_{12a}}-\underbrace{2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma\cdot(u^{\alpha}\partial^{\gamma}w^{\lambda}\partial_{\alpha}\partial_{\gamma}u_{\lambda})dx}_{\equiv\mathrm{I}_{12b}}-\underbrace{2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma\cdot(c_{s}^{-2}\partial_{\alpha}\partial_{\lambda}h\partial^{\alpha}w^{\lambda})dx}_{\equiv\mathrm{I}_{12c}}.\end{split}

(3.120)

The above integrals will be discussed as follows. By (
3.119
) and Hölder’s inequality, we obtain

\begin{split}|\mathrm{I}_{12a}|\lesssim&\|\Gamma\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|\Psi\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{x}})\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{H^{s-1}_{x}}\\
&+(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{x}})(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{3}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{4}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}).\end{split}

(3.121)

For
\mathrm{I}_{12b}
, we may not bound it directly like
\mathrm{I}_{12a}
. We decompose

\begin{split}\mathrm{I}_{12b}=&-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma\cdot(u^{0}\partial^{\gamma}w^{\lambda}\partial_{t}\partial_{\gamma}u_{\lambda})dx-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma\cdot(u^{i}\partial^{\gamma}w^{\lambda}\partial_{i}\partial_{\gamma}u_{\lambda})dx\\
=&\underbrace{-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma\cdot(u^{0}\partial^{0}w^{\lambda}\partial^{2}_{t}u_{\lambda})dx}_{\equiv\mathrm{I}_{12b1}}\underbrace{-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma\cdot(u^{0}\partial^{i}w^{\lambda}\partial_{t}\partial_{i}u_{\lambda})dx}_{\equiv\mathrm{I}_{12b2}}\underbrace{-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma\cdot(u^{i}\partial^{\gamma}w^{\lambda}\partial_{i}\partial_{\gamma}u_{\lambda})dx}_{\equiv\mathrm{I}_{12b3}}.\end{split}

(3.122)

Inserting (
3.55
) to
\mathrm{I}_{12b1}
, we derive that

\begin{split}\mathrm{I}_{12b1}=&\underbrace{-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma\cdot u^{0}\partial_{t}w^{\lambda}g^{0i}\partial^{2}_{ti}u_{\lambda}dx}_{\equiv\mathrm{I}_{12ba}}\underbrace{-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma\cdot u^{0}\partial_{t}w^{\lambda}g^{ij}\partial^{2}_{ij}u_{\lambda}dx}_{\equiv\mathrm{I}_{12bb}}\\
&\underbrace{+2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma\cdot u^{0}\partial_{t}w^{\lambda}Q_{\lambda}dx}_{\equiv\mathrm{I}_{12bc}}\underbrace{-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma\cdot u^{0}\partial_{t}w^{\lambda}c_{s}^{2}\Omega\mathrm{e}^{-h}W_{\lambda}dx}_{\equiv\mathrm{I}_{12bd}}.\end{split}

(3.123)

For
\mathrm{I}_{12ba}
, we can compute out

\begin{split}\mathrm{I}_{12ba}=&-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma\cdot\partial_{i}(u^{0}\partial_{t}w^{\lambda}g^{0i}\partial_{t}u_{\lambda})dx+2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma\cdot\partial_{i}u^{0}\partial_{t}w^{\lambda}g^{0i}\partial_{t}u_{\lambda}dx\\
&+2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma\cdot u^{0}\partial_{i}\partial_{t}w^{\lambda}g^{0i}\partial_{t}u_{\lambda}dx+2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma\cdot u^{0}\partial_{t}w^{\lambda}\partial_{i}g^{0i}\partial_{t}u_{\lambda}dx.\end{split}

Using the Plancherel formula and Hölder’s inequality, we can bound
\mathrm{I}_{12ba}
by

\begin{split}|\mathrm{I}_{12ba}|\lesssim&\|\Gamma\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|d\mathbf{w}d\mathbf{u}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\|\Gamma\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|d\mathbf{u}\nabla d\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|\Gamma\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\cdot d\mathbf{w}\cdot(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{x}})\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{H^{s-1}_{x}}\\
&+(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{x}})(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{4}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}).\end{split}

(3.124)

Similarly, we can calculate
\mathrm{I}_{12bb}
by

\begin{split}\mathrm{I}_{12bb}=&-2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma\cdot\partial_{i}(u^{0}\partial_{t}w^{\lambda}g^{ij}\partial_{j}u_{\lambda})dx+2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma\cdot\partial_{i}u^{0}\partial_{t}w^{\lambda}g^{ij}\partial_{j}u_{\lambda}dx\\
&+2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma\cdot u^{0}\partial_{i}\partial_{t}w^{\lambda}g^{ij}\partial_{j}u_{\lambda}dx+2\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma\cdot u^{0}\partial_{i}\partial_{t}w^{\lambda}\partial_{i}g^{ij}\partial_{j}u_{\lambda}dx.\end{split}

Using the Plancherel formula and Hölder’s inequality again, we can bound

\begin{split}|\mathrm{I}_{12bb}|\lesssim&\|\Gamma\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|d\mathbf{w}d\mathbf{u}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}}}+\|\Gamma\|_{L^{2}_{x}}(\|d\mathbf{u}d\mathbf{w}d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|d\mathbf{u}\nabla d\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{x}})\\
\lesssim&\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{x}})\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{H^{s-1}_{x}}\\
&+(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{x}})(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{4}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}).\end{split}

(3.125)

By Hölder’s inequality, we can estimate

and

\begin{split}|\mathrm{I}_{12bc}|\lesssim&\|\Gamma\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\cdot(d\mathbf{u},dh)\cdot d\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\\

(3.126)

and

\begin{split}|\mathrm{I}_{12bd}|\lesssim&\|\Gamma\|_{L^{2}_{x}}(\|d\mathbf{w}d\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}+\|d\mathbf{u}dhd\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{x}})\\

\begin{split}&\|\mathbf{w}(t,\cdot)\|^{2}_{\dot{H}^{2}_{x}}-\frac{C}{10(1+C)}\|\mathbf{w}(t,\cdot)\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{x}}-\frac{1}{10}\|\mathbf{w}(t,\cdot)\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\|\mathring{\mathbf{u}}_{0}\|^{6}_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|h_{0}\|^{6}_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|^{6}_{{H}^{2}_{x}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{3}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{4}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{10}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}\\
&+\|h_{0},\mathring{\mathbf{u}}_{0}\|^{2}_{H_{x}^{s}}+{\int^{t}_{0}}\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}})(1+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}})d\tau\\
&+{\int^{t}_{0}}\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}})(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{3}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{4}_{H^{s-1}_{x}})d\tau\\
&+\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|^{2}_{{H}^{2}_{x}}+{\int^{t}_{0}}(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{x}})(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{4}_{H^{s-1}_{x}})d\tau,\end{split}

(3.145)

By (
3.85
), (
3.145
), (
3.79
), and (
3.7
), it follows

\begin{split}&\|(h,\mathring{\mathbf{u}})\|^{2}_{{H}^{s}_{x}}(t)+\|u^{0}-1\|^{2}_{{H}^{s}_{x}}(t)+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{{H}^{2}_{x}}(t)-\frac{C}{10(1+C)}\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{x}}(t)-\frac{1}{10}\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{x}}(t)\\
\lesssim&\|\mathring{\mathbf{u}}_{0}\|^{2}_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|h_{0}\|^{2}_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|^{2}_{{H}^{2}_{x}}+\|\mathring{\mathbf{u}}_{0}\|^{6}_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|h_{0}\|^{6}_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|^{6}_{{H}^{2}_{x}}\\
&+\textstyle{\sup}_{[0,t]}\left\{\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{3}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{4}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{10}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}\right\}\\
&+{\int^{t}_{0}}\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{x}})(1+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s-1}_{x}})d\tau\\
&+{\int^{t}_{0}}\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{x}})(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{3}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{4}_{H^{s-1}_{x}})d\tau\\
&+{\int^{t}_{0}}(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{x}})(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{4}_{H^{s-1}_{x}})d\tau.\end{split}

(3.146)

Since the left hand side of (
3.146
)
\geq\frac{1}{2}\|(h,\mathring{\mathbf{u}})\|^{2}_{{H}^{s}_{x}}(t)+\frac{1}{2}\|u^{0}-1\|^{2}_{{H}^{s}_{x}}(t)+\frac{1}{2}\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{{H}^{2}_{x}}(t)
, so we have

\begin{split}\tilde{E}_{s}(t)\lesssim&\|\mathring{\mathbf{u}}_{0}\|^{2}_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|h_{0}\|^{2}_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|^{2}_{{H}^{2}_{x}}+\|\mathring{\mathbf{u}}_{0}\|^{6}_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|h_{0}\|^{6}_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|^{6}_{{H}^{2}_{x}}\\
&+\textstyle{\sup}_{[0,t]}\left\{\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{3}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{4}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{10}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}\right\}\\
&+{\int^{t}_{0}}\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{x}})(1+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s-1}_{x}})d\tau\\
&+{\int^{t}_{0}}\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{x}})(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{3}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{4}_{H^{s-1}_{x}})d\tau\\
&+{\int^{t}_{0}}(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{x}})(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{4}_{H^{s-1}_{x}})d\tau.\end{split}

(3.147)

By using (
3.1
), (
3.3
), (
3.4
), and (
3.147
), we have

\begin{split}\tilde{E}_{s}(t)\lesssim&\tilde{E}_{s}(0)+\tilde{E}_{s}(0)^{3}+(\tilde{E}_{s}(0)^{\frac{3}{2}}+\tilde{E}_{s}(0)^{2}+\tilde{E}_{s}(0)^{5})\exp(5\int^{t}_{0}\|d\mathbf{u},dh\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}d\tau)\\
&+{\int^{t}_{0}}\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\tilde{E}_{s}(\tau)d\tau+{\int^{t}_{0}}\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\tilde{E}_{s}(0)\exp(\int^{\tau}_{0}\|d\mathbf{u},dh\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}dz)\tilde{E}_{s}(\tau)d\tau\\
&+{\int^{t}_{0}}\tilde{E}_{s}(\tau)\left\{\tilde{E}_{s}(0)\exp(\int^{\tau}_{0}\|d\mathbf{u},dh\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}dz)+\tilde{E}_{s}(0)^{2}\exp(2\int^{\tau}_{0}\|d\mathbf{u},dh\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}dz)\right\}d\tau\\
&+{\int^{t}_{0}}\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}(\tilde{E}_{s}(0)^{\frac{3}{2}}+\tilde{E}_{s}(0)^{2})\exp(2\int^{\tau}_{0}\|d\mathbf{u},dh\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}dz)\tilde{E}_{s}(\tau)d\tau.\end{split}

Observing
\tilde{E}_{s}(0)^{\frac{3}{2}},\tilde{E}_{s}(0)^{2},\tilde{E}_{s}(0)^{3}\leq\max\{\tilde{E}_{s}(0),\tilde{E}_{s}(0)^{5}\}
, so we have

\begin{split}\tilde{E}_{s}(t)\lesssim&\tilde{E}_{s}(0)+\tilde{E}_{s}(0)^{5}+(\tilde{E}_{s}(0)+\tilde{E}_{s}(0)^{5})\exp(5\int^{t}_{0}\|d\mathbf{u},dh\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}d\tau)+{\int^{t}_{0}}\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\tilde{E}_{s}(\tau)d\tau\\
&+{\int^{t}_{0}}\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}(\tilde{E}_{s}(0)+\tilde{E}_{s}(0)^{5})\exp(2\int^{t}_{0}\|d\mathbf{u},dh\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}dz)E_{s}(\tau)d\tau\\
&+{\int^{t}_{0}}\tilde{E}_{s}(\tau)\left\{\tilde{E}_{s}(0)\exp(\int^{t}_{0}\|d\mathbf{u},dh\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}dz)+\tilde{E}_{s}(0)^{2}\exp(2\int^{t}_{0}\|d\mathbf{u},dh\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}dz)\right\}d\tau.\end{split}

Therefore, (
3.82
)-(
3.83
) can be immediately obtained after an application of Gronwall’s inequality.
∎

We next introduce a higher-order energy estimate as follows.

Corollary 3.4
.

Let
(h,\mathbf{u})
be a solution of (
1.13
). Let
2<s<\frac{5}{2}
. Let
\mathbf{w}
be defined in (
1.15
). Then the following energy estimates hold:

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}(t)\lesssim\mathbb{E}_{0}\exp\left\{18\int^{t}_{0}\|(dh,d\mathbf{u})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}d\tau\cdot\exp(3\int^{t}_{0}\|(dh,d\mathbf{u})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}d\tau)\right\},\end{split}

(3.148)

where
\mathbb{E}(t)
and
\mathbb{E}_{0}
are defined by

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}(t)=&\|(h,u^{0}-1,\mathring{\mathbf{u}})\|^{2}_{H_{x}^{s+1}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{H_{x}^{3}},\\
\mathbb{E}_{0}=&\|(h_{0},\mathring{\mathbf{u}}_{0})\|^{2}_{H_{x}^{s+1}}+\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|^{2}_{H_{x}^{3}}+\|(\mathring{\mathbf{u}}_{0},h_{0})\|^{3}_{H^{3}}+\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|^{2}_{{H}_{x}^{3}}\|h_{0}\|^{2}_{{H}_{x}^{3}}+\|(\mathring{\mathbf{u}}_{0},h_{0})\|^{6}_{H^{3}}.\end{split}

Proof.

By using (
3.1
) and (
3.2
), we have

\|(h,u^{0}-1,\mathring{\mathbf{u}})\|^{2}_{H_{x}^{s+1}}(t)\leq C\|(h_{0},\mathring{\mathbf{u}}_{0})\|^{2}_{H_{x}^{s+1}}\exp\{\int^{t}_{0}\|(dh,d\mathbf{u})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}d\tau\}.

(3.149)

By (
3.11
), we get

\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{L_{x}^{2}}\leq\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|^{2}_{L_{x}^{2}}+C\int^{t}_{0}\|d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}d\tau.

(3.150)

For
\|\mathbf{w}\|_{\dot{H}^{3}}
, we notice

\|\mathbf{w}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{3}}=\|\Delta\mathbf{w}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{1}}.

(3.151)

Recall
\mathring{\mathbf{w}}=(w^{1},w^{2},w^{3})
. Let us first consider
\|\mathring{\mathbf{w}}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}}}
. Let
\Delta_{H}
be defined in (
3.13
). Using (
1.18
), (
2.99
), and Lemma
2.4
, it follows

\begin{split}\|\Delta_{H}\mathring{\mathbf{w}}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{1}}\lesssim&\|\mathbf{u}\cdot\mathbf{W}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{2}}+\|\mathbf{w}\cdot(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{1}}+\|d\mathbf{u}\cdot\nabla\mathring{\mathbf{w}}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{1}}\\
\lesssim&\|\mathbf{W}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{2}}+\|\mathring{\mathbf{w}}\cdot(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{2}}+\|d\mathbf{u}\cdot\nabla\mathring{\mathbf{w}}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{1}}\\
\lesssim&\|\mathbf{W}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{2}}+\|\mathring{\mathbf{w}}\|_{{H}_{x}^{2}}\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{{H}_{x}^{2}}.\end{split}

(3.152)

By (
3.13
), we can see

\|\Delta_{H}\mathring{\mathbf{w}}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{1}}\lesssim\|\Delta\mathring{\mathbf{w}}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{1}}\lesssim\|\Delta_{H}\mathring{\mathbf{w}}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{1}}.

(3.153)

Combining (
3.152
) and (
3.153
), we can obtain

\begin{split}\|\Delta\mathring{\mathbf{w}}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{1}}\lesssim&\|\mathbf{W}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{2}}+\|\mathring{\mathbf{w}}\|_{{H}_{x}^{2}}\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{{H}_{x}^{2}}.\end{split}

(3.154)

Seeing from (
2.99
) and (
3.154
), we can obtain

\begin{split}\|\Delta{w^{0}}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{1}}\lesssim&\|\Delta\mathring{\mathbf{w}}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{1}}+\|\Delta\mathbf{u}\cdot\mathring{\mathbf{w}}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{1}}+\|\nabla\mathring{\mathbf{w}}\cdot\nabla\mathbf{u}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{1}}\\
\lesssim&\|\mathbf{W}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{2}}+\|\mathring{\mathbf{w}}\|_{{H}_{x}^{2}}\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{{H}_{x}^{2}}.\end{split}

(3.155)

Adding (
3.154
) and (
3.155
), it follows

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{w}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{3}}\lesssim\|\mathbf{W}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{2}}+\|\mathring{\mathbf{w}}\|_{{H}_{x}^{2}}\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{{H}_{x}^{2}}.\end{split}

(3.156)

Next, let us bound the term
\|\mathbf{W}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{2}}
. Operating
\Lambda_{x}^{2}
on (
2.15
) and multiplying
(u^{0})^{-1}\Lambda_{x}^{2}\mathbf{W}
on (
2.15
), we therefore get the energy estimate

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{W}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{2}}\lesssim&\|\mathbf{W}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{3}}+\int^{t}_{0}\|d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|d\mathbf{w}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{2}}\|\mathbf{W}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{2}}d\tau\\
&+\int^{t}_{0}\|d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\mathbf{W}\|^{2}_{\dot{H}_{x}^{2}}d\tau+\int^{t}_{0}\|\mathbf{w}\cdot d\mathbf{u}\cdot dh\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|\mathbf{W}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{2}}d\tau.\end{split}

(3.157)

Due to (
3.150
), (
3.156
), (
3.157
), and (
1.18
), it yields

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{{H}_{x}^{3}}\leq&C\|\mathbf{W}_{0}\|^{2}_{\dot{H}_{x}^{3}}+C\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|^{2}_{L_{x}^{2}}+C\int^{t}_{0}\|d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|d\mathbf{w}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{2}}\|\mathbf{W}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{2}}d\tau\\
&+C\int^{t}_{0}\|d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\mathbf{W}\|^{2}_{\dot{H}_{x}^{2}}d\tau+C\int^{t}_{0}\|d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{L_{x}^{2}}d\tau\\
&+C\int^{t}_{0}\|\mathbf{w}\cdot d\mathbf{u}\cdot dh\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|\mathbf{W}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{2}}d\tau+C\|\mathring{\mathbf{w}}\|^{2}_{{H}_{x}^{2}}\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{{H}_{x}^{2}}\\
\leq&C\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|^{2}_{{H}_{x}^{3}}+C\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|^{2}_{{H}_{x}^{3}}\|h_{0}\|^{2}_{{H}_{x}^{3}}+C\|{\mathbf{w}}\|^{2}_{{H}_{x}^{2}}\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{{H}_{x}^{2}}\\
&+C\int^{t}_{0}\|d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{{H}_{x}^{3}}(1+\|dh,d\mathbf{u}\|^{2}_{{H}_{x}^{2}}+\|dh,d\mathbf{u}\|^{3}_{{H}_{x}^{2}})d\tau.\end{split}

(3.158)

Because of Young’s inequality, we have

\begin{split}C\|{\mathbf{w}}\|^{2}_{{H}_{x}^{2}}\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{{H}_{x}^{2}}\leq&\frac{1}{2}\|{\mathbf{w}}\|^{2}_{{H}_{x}^{3}}+C\|{\mathbf{w}}\|^{2}_{{H}_{x}^{1}}\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{4}_{{H}_{x}^{2}}\\
\leq&\frac{1}{2}\|{\mathbf{w}}\|^{2}_{{H}_{x}^{3}}+C\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{6}_{{H}_{x}^{2}}.\end{split}

(3.159)

Inserting (
3.159
) to (
3.158
), we obtain

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{{H}_{x}^{3}}\lesssim&\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|^{2}_{{H}_{x}^{3}}+\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|^{2}_{{H}_{x}^{3}}\|h_{0}\|^{2}_{{H}_{x}^{3}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{6}_{{H}_{x}^{2}}\\
&+\int^{t}_{0}\|d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{{H}_{x}^{3}}(1+\|dh,d\mathbf{u}\|^{2}_{{H}_{x}^{2}}+\|dh,d\mathbf{u}\|^{3}_{{H}_{x}^{2}})d\tau\\
\lesssim&\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|^{2}_{{H}_{x}^{3}}+\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|^{2}_{{H}_{x}^{3}}\|h_{0}\|^{2}_{{H}_{x}^{3}}+\|(\mathring{\mathbf{u}}_{0},h_{0})\|^{6}_{H^{3}}\exp(6\|dh,d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}})\\
&+\int^{t}_{0}\|d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{{H}_{x}^{3}}(1+\|h,\mathring{\mathbf{u}}\|^{2}_{{H}_{x}^{2}}+\|h,\mathring{\mathbf{u}}\|^{3}_{{H}_{x}^{3}})d\tau.\end{split}

(3.160)

Above, we also use Lemma
2.14
, (
1.1
), and (
3.149
).

Finally, for (
3.160
), by Gronwall’s inequality, we have

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{{H}_{x}^{3}}\lesssim&(\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|^{2}_{{H}_{x}^{3}}+\|(\mathring{\mathbf{u}}_{0},h_{0})\|^{2}_{H^{3}}+\|(\mathring{\mathbf{u}}_{0},h_{0})\|^{3}_{H^{3}}+\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|^{2}_{{H}_{x}^{3}}\|h_{0}\|^{2}_{{H}_{x}^{3}}+\|(\mathring{\mathbf{u}}_{0},h_{0})\|^{6}_{H^{3}})\\
&\cdot\exp\{(6\int^{t}_{0}\|dh,d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}d\tau)(1+\mathrm{e}^{2\int^{t}_{0}\|dh,d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}d\tau}+\mathrm{e}^{3\int^{t}_{0}\|dh,d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}d\tau}\}\\
\lesssim&(\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|^{2}_{{H}_{x}^{3}}+\|(\mathring{\mathbf{u}}_{0},h_{0})\|^{2}_{H^{3}}+\|(\mathring{\mathbf{u}}_{0},h_{0})\|^{3}_{H^{3}}+\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|^{2}_{{H}_{x}^{3}}\|h_{0}\|^{2}_{{H}_{x}^{3}}+\|(\mathring{\mathbf{u}}_{0},h_{0})\|^{6}_{H^{3}})\\
&\cdot\exp\{18\int^{t}_{0}\|(dh,d\mathbf{u})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}d\tau\cdot\exp(3\int^{t}_{0}\|(dh,d\mathbf{u})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}d\tau)\}.\end{split}

(3.161)

Adding (
3.149
) and (
3.161
), we have proved (
3.148
).
∎

Based on the above energy theorems, we can obtain the uniqueness of solutions.

3.3.
Uniqueness of the solution

Corollary 3.5
.

Assume
2<s_{0}<s\leq\frac{5}{2}
and (
1.34
). Suppose
(h_{1},\mathbf{u}_{1})
and
(h_{2},\mathbf{u}_{2})
to be two solutions of (
1.13
) with the same initial data
(h_{0},\mathring{\mathbf{u}}_{0})\in H^{s}\times H^{s}
. We assume the initial modified vorticity
\mathbf{w}_{0}=-\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}\mathrm{e}^{h_{0}}u_{0\beta}\partial_{\gamma}u_{0\delta}\in H^{s_{0}}
. Then there exists a constant
T>0
such that

(h_{1},\mathring{\mathbf{u}}_{1},h_{2},\mathring{\mathbf{u}}_{2})\in C([0,T],H_{x}^{s})\cap C^{1}([0,T],H_{x}^{s-1})
,
\mathbf{w}\in C([0,T],H_{x}^{s_{0}})\cap C^{1}([0,T],H_{x}^{s_{0}-1})
and
(dh_{1},d\mathbf{u}_{1},dh_{2},d\mathbf{u}_{2})\in{L^{2}_{[0,T]}L^{\infty}_{x}}
. Furthermore, we have

\mathbf{u}_{1}=\mathbf{u}_{2},\quad h_{1}=h_{2}.

Proof.

For
0<t\leq T
, by Theorem
3.2
, and using Strichartz estimates
(dh_{1},d\mathbf{u}_{1},dh_{2},d\mathbf{u}_{2})\in{L^{2}_{[0,t]}L^{\infty}_{x}}
, we can bound

\|\mathbf{u}_{1}-\mathbf{u}_{2},h_{1}-h_{2}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}(t)\lesssim\|\mathbf{u}_{1}-\mathbf{u}_{2},h_{1}-h_{2}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}(0)=0.

So the solution is unique.
∎

Similarly, we can obtain the following corollary.

Corollary 3.6
.

Assume
2<s\leq\frac{5}{2}
and (
1.34
) hold. Suppose
(h_{1},\mathbf{u}_{1})
and
(h_{2},\mathbf{u}_{2})
to be two solutions of (
1.13
) with the same initial data
(h_{0},\mathring{\mathbf{u}}_{0})\in H^{s}\times H^{s}
. We also assume the initial modified vorticity
\mathbf{w}_{0}=-\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}\mathrm{e}^{h_{0}}u_{0\beta}\partial_{\gamma}u_{0\delta}\in H^{2}
. Then there exists a constant
T^{*}>0
such that

(\mathring{\mathbf{u}}_{1},h_{1},\mathring{\mathbf{u}}_{2},h_{2})\in C([0,T^{*}],H_{x}^{s})\cap C^{1}([0,T^{*}],H_{x}^{s-1})
,
\mathbf{w}\in C([0,T^{*}],H_{x}^{2})\cap C^{1}([0,T^{*}],H_{x}^{1})
and
(dh_{1},d\mathbf{u}_{1},dh_{2},d\mathbf{u}_{2})\in{L^{2}_{[0,T^{*}]}L^{\infty}_{x}}
. Furthermore, we have

\mathbf{u}_{1}=\mathbf{u}_{2},\quad h_{1}=h_{2}.

4.
Proof of Theorem
1.2

In this section, firstly, we reduce Theorem
1.2
to the case of smooth initial
data, i.e. Proposition
4.1
. Secondly, taking advantage of scaling and the finite speed of propagation to further simplify the problem to the case of smooth, small, supported initial
data, i.e. Proposition
4.2
. Finally, we reduce the result to a bootstrap argument, i.e. Proposition
4.3
.

4.1.
Reduction to smooth initial data

We state a proposition as follows.

Proposition 4.1
.

Let
2<s_{0}<s\leq\frac{5}{2}
. For each
M_{0}>0
, there exists
T,M>0
(depending on
C_{0},c_{0},s,s_{0}
and
M_{0}
) such that, for each smooth initial data
13
13

13

Here
\mathbf{w}_{0}=\mathrm{vort}(\mathrm{e}^{h_{0}}\mathbf{u}_{0})=-\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}\mathrm{e}^{h_{0}}u_{0\beta}\partial_{\gamma}u_{0\delta}
,
{\mathbf{u}}_{0}=(u^{0}_{0},\mathring{\mathbf{u}}_{0})^{\mathrm{T}}
.

(h_{0},{\mathbf{u}}_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0})
, which satisfies

\begin{split}&\|h_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{u}_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}}}\leq M_{0},\end{split}

(4.1)

there exists a smooth solution
(h,\mathbf{u},\mathbf{w})
to (
1.20
) on
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
satisfying

\begin{split}&\|h,u^{0}-1,\mathring{\mathbf{u}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T]}H_{x}^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T]}H_{x}^{s_{0}}}\leq M,\\
&u^{0}\geq 1,\quad\|u^{0}-1,\mathring{\mathbf{u}},h\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}}\leq 1+C_{0}.\end{split}

(4.2)

Furthermore, the solution satisfies estimates

\mathrm{(1)}
dispersive estimate for
\mathbf{u}
,
h
and
\mathbf{u}_{+}

\|d\mathbf{u},dh\|_{L^{2}_{[0,T]}C^{\delta}_{x}}+\|\nabla\mathbf{u}_{+},dh,d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,T]}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\leq M,

(4.3)

\mathrm{(2)}
Let
f
satisfy the equation (
1.41
). For each
1\leq r\leq s+1
, the Cauchy problem (
1.41
) is well-posed in
H_{x}^{r}\times H_{x}^{r-1}
. Furthermore, the following energy estimate

\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T]}H^{r}_{x}}+\|\partial_{t}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T]}H^{r-1}_{x}}\leq C_{M_{0}}(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}}),

(4.4)

and Strichartz estimates

\|\left<\nabla\right>^{k}f\|_{L^{2}_{[0,T]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq C_{M_{0}}(\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}}),\ \ \ k<r-1,

(4.5)

hold. The same estimates hold with
\left<\nabla\right>^{k}
replaced by
\left<\nabla\right>^{k-1}d
. Above,
C_{M_{0}}
is also a constant depending on
C_{0},c_{0},s,s_{0},M_{0}
.

Next, we show that Theorem
1.2
is a consequence of Proposition
4.1
.

Proof of Theorem
1.2
by using Proposition
4.1
.

Let
(h_{0},{\mathbf{u}}_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0})
be stated in Theorem
1.2
. So we have

\|\mathring{\mathbf{u}}_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|h_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}}}\leq M_{0}.

Let
\{(h_{0k},\mathring{\mathbf{u}}_{0k})\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}}
be a sequence of smooth data converging to
(h_{0},\mathring{\mathbf{u}}_{0})
in
H^{s}\times H^{s}
. Referring (
1.1
), we set

u^{0}_{0k}=\sqrt{1+|\mathring{\mathbf{u}}_{0k}|^{2}},\quad{\mathbf{u}}_{0k}=(u^{0}_{0k},\mathring{\mathbf{u}}_{0k}).

Referring (
1.15
), we also define
\mathbf{w}_{0k}=\textrm{vort}(\mathrm{e}^{h_{0k}}\mathbf{u}_{0k})
. Therefore,
\{(h_{0k},{\mathbf{u}}_{0k},\mathbf{w}_{0k})\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}}
is a smooth sequence which converges to
(h_{0},{\mathbf{u}}_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0})
in
H^{s}\times H^{s}\times H^{s_{0}}
. By Proposition
4.1
, for every
k\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}
, there exists a solution
(h_{k},\mathbf{u}_{k},\mathbf{w}_{k})
to System (
1.20
) with

(h_{k},\mathbf{u}_{k},\mathbf{w}_{k})|_{t=0}=(h_{0k},\mathbf{u}_{0k},\mathbf{w}_{0k}).

Note that the solution of (
1.20
) also satisfies (
1.13
) and (
2.12
).

Set
\mathbf{U}_{k}=(p_{k},u_{1k},u_{2k},u_{3k})^{\mathrm{T}},k\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}
, where
p_{k}=p(h_{k})
. For
j\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}
, we then have

\begin{split}&A^{0}(\mathbf{U}_{k})\partial_{t}\mathbf{U}_{k}+\sum^{3}_{i=1}A^{i}(\mathbf{U}_{k})\partial_{i}\mathbf{U}_{k}=0,\\
&A^{0}(\mathbf{U}_{l})\partial_{t}\mathbf{U}_{l}+\sum^{3}_{i=1}A^{i}(\mathbf{U}_{l})\partial_{i}\mathbf{U}_{l}=0.\end{split}

By standard energy estimates, we can carry out

\frac{d}{dt}\|\mathbf{U}_{k}-\mathbf{U}_{l}\|_{H_{x}^{s-1}}\leq C_{k,l}\left(\|d\mathbf{U}_{k},d\mathbf{U}_{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\mathbf{U}_{k}-\mathbf{U}_{l}\|_{H_{x}^{s-1}}+\|\mathbf{U}_{k}-\mathbf{U}_{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\nabla\mathbf{U}_{l}\|_{H_{x}^{s-1}}\right),

where
C_{k,l}
depends on the
L^{\infty}_{x}
norm of
\mathbf{U}_{k}
and
\mathbf{U}_{l}
. Due to the Strichartz estimates of
d\mathbf{u}_{k}
and
dh_{k}

(k\in\mathbb{Z}^{+})
in Proposition
4.1
, we can derive that

\begin{split}\|(\mathbf{U}_{k}-\mathbf{U}_{l})(t,\cdot)\|_{H_{x}^{s-1}}&\lesssim\|(\mathbf{U}_{k}-\mathbf{U}_{l})(0,\cdot)\|_{H_{x}^{s-1}}\lesssim\|(\mathbf{u}_{0k}-\mathbf{u}_{0l},h_{0k}-h_{0l})\|_{H^{s-1}}.\end{split}

So
\{(p_{k},u_{1k},u_{2k},u_{3k})\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}}
is a Cauchy sequence in
C([0,T];H_{x}^{s-1})
. Denote
(p,u_{1},u_{2},u_{3})
being the limit. Then
(p,u_{1},u_{2},u_{3})\in C([0,T];H_{x}^{s-1})
and

\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}(p_{k},u_{1k},u_{2k},u_{3k})=(p,u_{1},u_{2},u_{3})\ \mathrm{in}\ C([0,T];H_{x}^{s-1}).

(4.6)

Note
u^{2}_{0k}-(u^{2}_{1k}+u^{2}_{2k}+u^{2}_{3k})=1
. By (
4.6
), we infer

\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}u_{0k}=u_{0}\ \mathrm{in}\ C([0,T];H_{x}^{s-1}),

and

u^{2}_{0}-(u^{2}_{1}+u^{2}_{2}+u^{2}_{3})=1.

By (
1.4
), (
1.8
), and (
1.11
), a direct calculation tells us

h=\int\frac{\vartheta\varrho^{\vartheta-1}}{\varrho^{\vartheta}+\varrho}d\varrho.

(4.7)

Due to (
4.6
) and Lemma
2.3
, we get

\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\varrho_{k}=\varrho\ \mathrm{in}\ C([0,T];H_{x}^{s-1}).

(4.8)

By (
4.7
), then
h_{k}
is also a function of
\varrho_{k}
. Combining with (
4.8
) and Lemma
2.3
, we infer

\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}h_{k}=h\ \mathrm{in}\ C([0,T];H_{x}^{s-1}).

Note
\mathbf{w}_{k}=\mathrm{vort}(\mathrm{e}^{h_{k}}\mathbf{u}_{k})
.
Due to product estimates and Lemma
2.3
, we have

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{w}_{k}-\mathbf{w}_{l}\|_{H_{x}^{s_{0}-2}}&\lesssim(\|\mathbf{u}_{k}-\mathbf{u}_{l}\|_{H_{x}^{s_{0}-1}}+\|h_{k}-h_{l}\|_{H_{x}^{s_{0}-1}})\\
&\lesssim(\|\mathbf{u}_{0k}-\mathbf{u}_{0l}\|_{H^{s}}+\|h_{0k}-h_{0l}\|_{H^{s}}).\end{split}

(4.9)

From (
4.9
),
\{\mathbf{w}_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}}
is a Cauchy sequence in
C([0,T];H_{x}^{s_{0}-2})
. Let
\mathbf{w}
be this limit. We have

\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\mathbf{w}_{k}=\mathbf{w}\ \mathrm{in}\ C([0,T];H_{x}^{s_{0}-2}).

Since
(h_{k},\mathbf{u}_{k})
is uniformly bounded in
C([0,T];H_{x}^{s})
and
\mathbf{w}_{k}
is bounded in
C([0,T];H_{x}^{s_{0}})
respectively. Thus,
(h,u_{1},u_{2},u_{3})\in C([0,T];H_{x}^{s})
and
\mathbf{w}\in C([0,T];H_{x}^{s_{0}})
.

By using Proposition
4.1
again, the sequence
\{(d\mathbf{u}_{k},dh_{k})\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}}
is uniformly bounded in
L^{2}([0,T];C_{x}^{\delta})
. Consequently, the sequence
\{(d\mathbf{u}_{k},dh_{k})\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}}
converges to
(d\mathbf{u},dh)
in
L^{2}([0,T];L_{x}^{\infty})
. That is,

\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}(d\mathbf{u}_{k},dh_{k})=(d\mathbf{u},dh).

(4.10)

On the other hand, we also deduce that the sequence
\{(d\mathbf{u}_{k},dh_{k})\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}}
is bounded in
L^{2}([0,T];\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2})
.
Combining (
4.10
) and (
4.3
), we get

\|d\mathbf{u},dh\|_{L^{2}_{[0,T]}L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|d\mathbf{u},dh\|_{L^{2}_{[0,T]}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\leq M.

(4.11)

By the fundamental formula of calculus, when
T\leq\frac{1}{(1+M)^{2}}
, we can obtain

\|u^{0}-1,\mathring{\mathbf{u}},h\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leq C_{0}+T^{\frac{1}{2}}\|d\mathbf{u},dh\|_{L^{2}_{[0,T]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq 1+C_{0}.

(4.12)

For

\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{u}_{+}+\mathbf{u}_{-},

we therefore have

\begin{split}\|\nabla\mathbf{u}_{+}\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\leq\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}+\|\nabla\mathbf{u}_{-}\|_{\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}.\end{split}

(4.13)

Note that
\mathbf{P}
is a space-time elliptic operator on
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
. By using the elliptic estimate and Sobolev inequality, we have

\begin{split}\|\nabla\mathbf{u}_{-}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,T]}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}&\lesssim\|\mathbf{P}^{-1}(\mathrm{e}^{h}\mathbf{W})\|_{L^{2}_{[0,T]}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{2}}_{2,2}}\lesssim\|\mathrm{e}^{h}\mathbf{W}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,T]}\ \dot{H}^{s_{0}-\frac{3}{2}}}\lesssim(1+\|h_{0}\|_{H^{s}})\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}}}.\end{split}

This implies
\nabla\mathbf{u}_{-}\in L^{2}([0,T];\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2})
. By (
4.13
), then
\nabla\mathbf{u}_{+}\in L^{2}([0,T];\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2})
. Therefore, we complete the proof of Theorem
1.2
.
∎

4.2.
Reduction to small, smooth, and compactly supported data

Denote by
c
the largest speed of
propagation corresponding to the acoustic metric
g
. We can further reduce the problem to this following
result:

Proposition 4.2
.

Let
2<s_{0}<s\leq\frac{5}{2}
. Assume (
1.35
)-(
1.36
) hold. Suppose the initial data
(h_{0},\mathring{\mathbf{u}}_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0})
be smooth, supported in
B(0,c+2)
and satisfying

\begin{split}&\|\mathring{\mathbf{u}}_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|h_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}}}\leq\epsilon_{3}.\end{split}

(4.14)

Then the Cauchy problem of (
1.20
) admits a smooth solution
(h,\mathbf{u},\mathbf{w})
on
[-2,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
. Moreover, it has the following properties:

\mathrm{(1)}
energy estimate

\begin{split}&\|\mathring{\mathbf{u}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-1,1]}H_{x}^{s}}+\|u^{0}-1\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-1,1]}H_{x}^{s}}+\|h\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-1,1]}H_{x}^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-1,1]}H_{x}^{s_{0}}}\leq\epsilon_{2},\\
&1\leq u^{0}\leq 1+C_{0},\quad\|\mathbf{u},h\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-1,1]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}}\leq 1+C_{0}.\end{split}

(4.15)

\mathrm{(2)}
dispersive estimate for
\mathbf{u}
and
h

\|d\mathbf{u},dh\|_{L^{2}_{[-1,1]}C^{\delta}_{x}}+\|dh,\nabla\mathbf{u}_{+},d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{2}_{[-1,1]}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\leq\epsilon_{2},

(4.16)

where
\mathbf{u}_{+}
is defined as in (
1.23
)-(
1.24
).

\mathrm{(3)}
dispersive estimate for the linear equation

Let
f
satisfy

\begin{cases}&\square_{g}f=0,\qquad(t,x)\in[-2,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\\
&(f,\partial_{t}f)|_{t=t_{0}}=(f_{0},f_{1})\in H^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\times H^{r-1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}),\end{cases}

(4.17)

where
g
is defined in (
1.17
)-(
1.16
). For each
1\leq r\leq s+1
, then the Cauchy problem (
4.17
) has a unique solution in
C([-2,2];H_{x}^{r})\cap C^{1}([-2,2];H_{x}^{r-1})
. Furthermore, for
k<r-1
, we also have

\|\left<\nabla\right>^{k}f\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\|f_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|f_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}},

(4.18)

and the same estimates hold with
\left<\nabla\right>^{k}
replaced by
\left<\nabla\right>^{k-1}d
.

Due to Proposition
4.2
, we are ready to prove Proposition
4.1
.

Proof of Proposition
4.1
by using Proposition
4.2
.

We divide the proof into three steps.

Step 1: Scaling
. Recall

\begin{split}&\|\mathring{\mathbf{u}}_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|h_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}}}\leq M_{0}.\end{split}

(4.19)

Taking the scaling

\begin{split}&\underline{{\mathbf{u}}}(t,x)=\mathbf{u}(Tt,Tx),\quad\underline{h}(t,x)=h(Tt,Tx),\end{split}

by (
4.19
), we can compute out

\begin{split}&\|\underline{\mathbf{u}}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{s}}=T^{s-\frac{3}{2}}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{s}}\leq M_{0}T^{s-\frac{3}{2}}.\end{split}

(4.20)

Similarly, we obtain

\begin{split}&\|\underline{h}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{s}}\leq M_{0}T^{s-\frac{3}{2}},\end{split}

(4.21)

and

\begin{split}\|\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}\partial_{\gamma}\underline{u}_{0\delta}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}}}&=T^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{2}}\|\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}\partial_{\gamma}{u}_{0\delta}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}}}\\
&=T^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{2}}\|\mathrm{e}^{-{h}_{0}}\mathbf{w}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}}},\\
&\leq M_{0}T^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{2}}.\end{split}

(4.22)

Set
\underline{\mathbf{w}}_{0}=(\underline{w}^{0}_{0},\underline{w}^{1}_{0},\underline{w}^{2}_{0},\underline{w}^{3}_{0})^{\mathrm{T}}
and
\underline{w}^{\alpha}_{0}=-\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}\underline{{u}}_{0\beta}\partial_{\gamma}(\mathrm{e}^{\underline{h}_{0}}\underline{{u}}_{0\delta})
. By (
4.22
), we have

\begin{split}\|\underline{\mathbf{w}}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}}}\leq M_{0}T^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{2}}.\end{split}

(4.23)

Choose sufficiently small
T
such that

\max\{M_{0}T^{s-\frac{3}{2}},M_{0}T^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{2}}\}\ll\epsilon_{3}.

(4.24)

Therefore, by (
4.20
), (
4.21
), (
4.23
), and (
4.24
), we get

\begin{split}&\|\underline{\mathbf{u}}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{s}}\leq\epsilon_{3},\qquad\|\underline{h}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{s}}\leq\epsilon_{3},\\
&\|\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}\partial_{\gamma}\underline{u}_{0\delta}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}}}\leq\epsilon_{3},\quad\|\underline{\mathbf{w}}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}}}\leq\epsilon_{3}.\end{split}

(4.25)

Using (
1.34
) and (
1.1
), we also have

\|\underline{\mathbf{u}}_{0},\underline{h}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C_{0},\quad\underline{u}^{0}_{0}\geq 1.

(4.26)

Next, to reduce the initial data with support set, we use the physical localization technique.

Step 2: Localization
. Note that the propagation speed of (
1.20
) is finite. Let
c
be the largest speed of (
1.20
). Set
\chi(x)
be a smooth function supported in
B(0,c+2)
, and which equals
1
in
B(0,c+1)
. To be simple, we set

\underline{\mathbf{u}}_{0}=(\underline{u}^{0}_{0},\mathring{\underline{\mathbf{u}}}_{0}).

For given
y\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we define the localized initial data for the velocity and density near
y
:

\begin{split}\mathring{\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}}_{0}(x)=&\chi(x-y)(\mathring{\underline{\mathbf{u}}}_{0}(x)-\mathring{\underline{\mathbf{u}}}_{0}(y)),\\
\widetilde{h}_{0}(x)=&\chi(x-y)(\underline{h}_{0}(x)-\underline{h}_{0}(y)).\end{split}

(4.27)

Since the velocity
\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}
satisfies (
1.1
), so we set

{\widetilde{u}^{0}}_{0}=\sqrt{1+|\mathring{\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}}_{0}|^{2}},\quad\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{0}=({\widetilde{u}^{0}}_{0},\mathring{\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}}_{0}).

(4.28)

By using (
4.26
), (
4.27
), and (
4.28
), we have

\|\mathring{\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}}_{0},\widetilde{h}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C_{0},\quad 1\leq{\widetilde{u}^{0}}_{0}\leq 1+C_{0}.

(4.29)

Since
s,s_{0}\in(2,\frac{5}{2})
, we can verify that

\|\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{0}\|_{{H}^{s}}\lesssim\|\underline{\mathbf{u}}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{s}},\quad\|\widetilde{h}_{0}\|_{H^{s}}\lesssim\|\underline{h}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{s}}.

(4.30)

We also need to define the specific vorticity:

\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{0}=-\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}\mathrm{e}^{\widetilde{h}_{0}+\widetilde{h}_{0}(y)}(\widetilde{u}_{0\beta}+\underline{u}_{0\beta}(y))\partial_{\gamma}\widetilde{u}_{0\delta}.

(4.31)

For
\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{0}
only depending on spatial variables, so (
4.31
) and (
4.29
) give us

\|\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{0}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|\nabla\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{0}\|_{L^{2}}.

(4.32)

Substituting (
4.27
) to (
4.31
), so we have

\begin{split}\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{0}=&-\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}\mathrm{e}^{\widetilde{h}_{0}}\widetilde{u}_{0\beta}\partial_{\gamma}\chi\cdot(\underline{u}_{0\delta}-\underline{u}_{0\delta}(y))-\chi^{2}\mathrm{e}^{\widetilde{h}_{0}}(\widetilde{u}_{0\beta}+\underline{u}_{0\beta}(y))\cdot\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}\partial_{\gamma}\underline{u}_{0\delta}.\end{split}

(4.33)

Due to (
4.33
), (
4.29
), and Hölder’s inequality, we infer

\begin{split}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}}}\lesssim&\|\underline{u}_{0\delta}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}}}+\|\underline{h}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}}}+\|\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}\partial_{\gamma}\underline{u}_{0\delta}\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}}}.\end{split}

(4.34)

Combining (
4.25
), (
4.30
), (
4.32
), and (
4.34
), we therefore have

\begin{split}&\|\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{0}\|_{{H}^{s}}+\|\widetilde{h}_{0}\|_{{H}^{s}}+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{0}\|_{{H}^{s_{0}}}\lesssim\epsilon_{3}.\end{split}

(4.35)

Step 3: Using Proposition
4.2
.
Under the initial conditions (
4.29
) and (
4.35
), by Proposition
4.2
, there is a smooth solution
(\widetilde{h},\widetilde{\mathbf{u}},\widetilde{\mathbf{w}})
on
[-2,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
such that

\begin{cases}\square_{\widetilde{g}}\widetilde{h}=\widetilde{D},\\
\square_{\widetilde{g}}\widetilde{u}^{\alpha}=-\widetilde{c}^{2}_{s}\widetilde{\Omega}\mathrm{e}^{-(\widetilde{h}+\underline{h}_{0}(y))}\widetilde{W}^{\alpha}+\widetilde{Q}^{\alpha},\\
(\widetilde{u}^{\kappa}+\underline{u}_{0}^{\kappa}(y))\partial_{\kappa}\widetilde{w}^{\alpha}=-(\widetilde{u}^{\alpha}+\underline{u}_{0}^{\alpha}(y))\widetilde{w}^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}\widetilde{h}+\widetilde{w}^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}\widetilde{u}^{\alpha}-\widetilde{w}^{\alpha}\partial_{\kappa}\widetilde{u}^{\kappa},\\
\partial_{\alpha}\widetilde{w}^{\alpha}=-\widetilde{w}^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}\widetilde{h},\\
(\widetilde{h},\widetilde{u},\widetilde{w})|_{t=0}=(\widetilde{h}_{0},\widetilde{u}_{0},\widetilde{w}_{0}),\end{cases}

(4.36)

where the quantities
\widetilde{c}^{2}_{s}
,
\widetilde{{g}}
,
\widetilde{\Omega}
, and
\widetilde{\mathbf{W}}=(\widetilde{W}^{0},\widetilde{W}^{1},\widetilde{W}^{2},\widetilde{W}^{3})^{\mathrm{T}}
are defined by

\begin{split}\widetilde{c}^{2}_{s}&=\frac{dp}{d{h}}(\widetilde{h}+\underline{h}_{0}(y)),\\
\widetilde{g}&=g(\widetilde{h}+\underline{h}_{0}(y),\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}+\underline{\mathbf{u}}_{0}(y)),\\
\widetilde{\Omega}&=\Omega(\widetilde{h}+\underline{h}_{0}(y),\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}+\underline{\mathbf{u}}_{0}(y)),\end{split}

(4.37)

and

\begin{split}\widetilde{W}^{\alpha}&=-\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}(\widetilde{u}_{\beta}+\underline{u}_{0\beta}(y))\partial_{\gamma}\widetilde{w}_{\delta}+\widetilde{c}_{s}^{-2}\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}(\widetilde{u}_{\beta}+\underline{u}_{0\beta}(y))\widetilde{w}_{\delta}\partial_{\gamma}\widetilde{h},\end{split}

(4.38)

and

\begin{split}\widetilde{Q}^{\alpha}=&\widetilde{Q}_{1\kappa}^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{h}\partial_{\gamma}\widetilde{u}^{\kappa}+\widetilde{Q}_{2\kappa\delta}^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{u}^{\kappa}\partial_{\gamma}u^{\delta}+\widetilde{Q}_{3}^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{h}\partial_{\gamma}\widetilde{h},\\
\widetilde{D}^{\alpha}=&\widetilde{D}_{1\kappa}^{\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{h}\partial_{\gamma}\widetilde{u}^{\kappa}+\widetilde{D}_{2\kappa\delta}^{\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{u}^{\kappa}\partial_{\gamma}\widetilde{u}^{\delta}+\widetilde{D}_{3}^{\beta\gamma}\partial_{\beta}\widetilde{h}\partial_{\gamma}\widetilde{h},\end{split}

and the functions
\widetilde{Q}_{1\kappa}^{\alpha\beta\gamma}
,
\widetilde{Q}_{2\kappa\delta}^{\alpha\beta\gamma}
,
\widetilde{Q}_{3}^{\alpha\beta\gamma}
,
\widetilde{D}_{1\kappa}^{\beta\gamma}
,
\widetilde{D}_{2\kappa\delta}^{\beta\gamma}
,
\widetilde{D}_{3}^{\beta\gamma}
have the same formulations with
{Q}_{1\kappa}^{\alpha\beta\gamma}
,
{Q}_{2\kappa\delta}^{\alpha\beta\gamma}
,
\widetilde{Q}_{3}^{\alpha\beta\gamma}
,
{D}_{1\kappa}^{\beta\gamma}
,
{D}_{2\kappa\delta}^{\beta\gamma}
,
{D}_{3}^{\beta\gamma}
by replacing
(\mathbf{u},{h})
to
(\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}+\underline{\mathbf{u}}_{0}(y),\widetilde{h}+\underline{h}_{0}(y))
.
To see a better component of the velocity
\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}
, we define

\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}=\mathrm{I}-\left[m^{\alpha\beta}+2(\widetilde{u}^{\alpha}+\underline{u}^{\alpha}_{0}(y))(\widetilde{u}^{\beta}+\underline{u}^{\beta}_{0}(y))\right]\partial^{2}_{\alpha\beta}.

(4.39)

Then
\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}
is a space-time elliptic operator (please see Lemma
9.2
). We next denote

\widetilde{u}^{\alpha}_{-}=\mathbf{P}^{-1}(\mathrm{e}^{\widetilde{h}+\underline{h}_{0}(y)}\widetilde{W}^{\alpha}),\quad\widetilde{u}_{+}=\widetilde{u}-\widetilde{u}_{-}.

(4.40)

and

\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}=\partial_{t}+\frac{\widetilde{u}^{i}+\underline{u}^{i}_{0}(y)}{\widetilde{u}^{0}+\underline{u}^{0}_{0}(y)}\partial_{i}.

(4.41)

By (
2.78
), so we infer

\begin{split}\square_{g}\widetilde{u}^{\alpha}_{+}&=\widetilde{\Omega}(\widetilde{c}^{2}_{s}+1)(\widetilde{u}^{\gamma}+\underline{u}^{\gamma}_{0}(y))(\widetilde{u}^{\beta}+\underline{u}^{\beta}_{0}(y))\partial^{2}_{\beta\gamma}\widetilde{u}^{\alpha}_{-}+\widetilde{\Omega}\widetilde{c_{s}}^{2}\widetilde{u}_{-}^{\alpha}+\widetilde{Q}^{\alpha}.\end{split}

Using Proposition
4.2
again, the solution
(\widetilde{h},\widetilde{{\mathbf{u}}},\widetilde{\mathbf{w}})
also satisfies

\|\mathring{\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s}}+\|\widetilde{u}^{0}-1\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s}}+\|\widetilde{h}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s}}+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s_{0}}}\leq\epsilon_{2},

(4.42)

and

\|d\widetilde{\mathbf{u}},d\widetilde{h}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}C^{\delta}_{x}}+\|d\widetilde{\mathbf{u}},\nabla\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{+},d\widetilde{h}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\leq\epsilon_{2}.

(4.43)

Moreover, the linear wave equation endowed with the Lorentz metric
\widetilde{{g}}

\begin{cases}&\square_{\widetilde{{g}}}\tilde{f}=0,\\
&\tilde{f}(t_{0},\cdot)=\widetilde{f}_{0},\ \partial_{t}\tilde{f}(t_{0},)=\widetilde{f}_{1},\end{cases}

(4.44)

also admits a solution
\widetilde{f}\in C([-2,2],H_{x}^{r})\times C^{1}([-2,2],H_{x}^{r-1})
, and if
k<r-1
, the following estimate

\|\left<\nabla\right>^{k}\widetilde{f}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\lesssim\|\widetilde{f}_{0}\|_{H^{r}}+\|\widetilde{f}_{1}\|_{H^{r-1}},

(4.45)

holds. On the other hand, if we set

\begin{split}\bar{h}(t,x)&=\widetilde{h}+\underline{h}_{0}(y),\\
\bar{{\mathbf{u}}}(t,x)&=(\bar{u}^{0},\mathring{\bar{\mathbf{u}}}),\quad\bar{u}^{0}=\sqrt{1+|\mathring{\bar{\mathbf{u}}}|^{2}},\\
\mathring{\bar{\mathbf{u}}}(t,x)&=\mathring{\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}}+\underline{\mathring{\mathbf{u}}}_{0}(y),\\
\bar{\mathbf{w}}(t,x)&=\mathrm{vort}(\mathrm{e}^{\bar{h}}\bar{\mathbf{u}}),\end{split}

(4.46)

then
(\bar{h},\bar{\mathbf{u}},\bar{\mathbf{w}})
is also a solution of (
4.36
), and its initial data coincides with
(h_{0},\mathbf{u}_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0})
in
B(y,c+1)
. Consider a restriction

(\bar{h},\bar{\mathbf{u}},\bar{\mathbf{w}})|_{\mathrm{K}^{y}},

(4.47)

where
y\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
and
\mathrm{K}^{y}=\left\{(t,x):ct+|x-y|\leq c+1,|t|<1\right\}
. Then (
4.47
) solves (
4.36
) on
\mathrm{K}^{y}
. By finite speed of propagation, a smooth solution
(\bar{h},\bar{\mathbf{u}},\bar{\mathbf{w}})
solves (
1.20
) on
[-1,1]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
. Without diffusion, we still use the notation
\bar{h},\bar{\mathbf{u}}
and
\bar{\mathbf{w}}
being the restrictions on
\mathrm{K}^{y}
.

From (
4.46
), by time-space scaling
(t,x)
to
(T^{-1}t,T^{-1}x)
, we also obtain

\begin{split}&(h,\mathbf{u},\mathbf{w})=(\bar{h},\bar{\mathbf{u}},\bar{\mathbf{w}})(T^{-1}t,T^{-1}x),\\
&(h,\mathbf{u},\mathbf{w})|_{t=0}=(\bar{h},\bar{\mathbf{u}},\bar{\mathbf{w}})(0,T^{-1}x)=(h_{0},\mathbf{u}_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0}).\end{split}

(4.48)

Therefore, the function
(h,\mathbf{u},\mathbf{w})
defined in (
4.48
) is the solution of (
1.20
) according to the uniqueness of solutions, i.e. Corollary
3.5
. To obtain the energy estimates for
(h,\mathbf{u},\mathbf{w})
on
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, let us use the cartesian grid
3^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{Z}^{3}
in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
, and a corresponding smooth partition of unity

\textstyle{\sum}_{y\in 3^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\psi(x-y)=1,

such that the function
\psi
is supported in the unit ball. Therefore, we have

\begin{split}&\mathring{\bar{\mathbf{u}}}=\textstyle{\sum}_{y\in 3^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\psi(x-y)(\mathring{\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}}+\underline{\mathring{\mathbf{u}}}_{0}(y)),\\
&\bar{h}=\textstyle{\sum}_{y\in 3^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\psi(x-y)(\widetilde{h}+\underline{h}_{0}(y)),\\
&\bar{u}^{0}=\sqrt{1+|\mathring{\bar{\mathbf{u}}}|^{2}},\quad\bar{\mathbf{u}}=(\bar{u}^{0},\mathring{\bar{\mathbf{u}}}),\quad\bar{\mathbf{w}}=\mathrm{vort}(\mathrm{e}^{\bar{h}}\bar{\mathbf{u}}).\end{split}

(4.49)

By (
4.43
) and (
4.49
), we can see

\begin{split}&\|d\bar{\mathbf{u}},d\bar{h}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,1]}C^{\delta}_{x}}+\|d\bar{\mathbf{u}},\nabla\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{+},d\bar{h}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,1]}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\\
\leq&\sup_{y\in 3^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{Z}^{3}}(\|d\widetilde{\mathbf{u}},d\widetilde{h}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,1]}{C^{\delta}_{x}}}+\|d\widetilde{\mathbf{u}},\nabla\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{+},d\widetilde{h}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,1]}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}})\\
\leq&C(\|\mathring{\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|\widetilde{h}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{s_{0}}}).\end{split}

Due to (
4.42
), (
4.26
), and (
4.49
), we shall obtain

\begin{split}&\|\bar{u}^{0}-1,\bar{\mathring{\mathbf{u}}},\bar{h}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,1]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq\|\widetilde{u}^{0}-1,\mathring{\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}},\widetilde{h}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|\underline{u}_{0}^{0}-1,\mathring{\underline{\mathbf{u}}}_{0},\underline{h}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq 1+C_{0}.\end{split}

(4.50)

By changing of coordinates and using
{\delta}\in(0,s-2)
and
2<s_{0}<s
, we get

\begin{split}&\|d{\mathbf{u}},d{h}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,T]}C^{\delta}_{x}}+\|d{\mathbf{u}},\nabla{\mathbf{u}}_{+},d{h}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,T]}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\\
\leq&T^{-(\frac{1}{2}+{\delta})}\|d\widetilde{\mathbf{u}},d\widetilde{h}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,1]}C^{\delta}_{x}}+\|d\widetilde{\mathbf{u}},\nabla\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{+},d\widetilde{h}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,1]}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\\
\leq&C(T^{-(\frac{1}{2}+{\delta})}+T^{-(\frac{1}{2}+s_{0}-2)})(\|\mathring{\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|\widetilde{h}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{s_{0}}})\\
\leq&C(T^{-(\frac{1}{2}+{\delta})}+T^{-(\frac{1}{2}+s_{0}-2)})(T^{s-\frac{3}{2}}\|{\mathbf{u}}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s}}+T^{s-\frac{3}{2}}\|h_{0}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s}}+T^{s_{0}+1-\frac{3}{2}}\|{\mathbf{w}}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{s_{0}}})\\
\leq&C(\|\mathring{\mathbf{u}}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|{h}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{s}}+\|{\mathbf{w}}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{s_{0}}}).\end{split}

(4.51)

By using (
4.50
) and changing of coordinates from
(t,x)\rightarrow(T^{-1}t,T^{-1}x)
, we get

\begin{split}&\|u^{0}-1,\mathring{\mathbf{u}},{h}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}}=\|\bar{u}^{0}-1,\mathring{\bar{\mathbf{u}}},\bar{h}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,1]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq 1+C_{0},\end{split}

(4.52)

and

\begin{split}&u^{0}=\sqrt{1+|\mathring{\mathbf{u}}|^{2}}\geq 1.\end{split}

(4.53)

Using Theorem
3.2
, (
4.51
), (
4.52
), and (
4.53
), we can obtain that
(h,\mathbf{u},\mathbf{w})
satisfies (
4.1
), (
4.2
) and (
4.3
). It remains for us to prove (
4.4
) and (
4.5
).

For
1\leq r\leq s+1
, we consider the following homogeneous linear wave equation

\begin{cases}\square_{{g}}f=0,\qquad[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\\
(f,f_{t})|_{t=0}=(f_{0},f_{1})\in H_{x}^{r}\times H^{r-1}_{x}.\end{cases}

(4.54)

For (
4.54
) is a scaling invariant system, so we can transfer (
4.54
) to the localized linear equation

\begin{cases}\square_{\widetilde{g}}{f}^{y}=0,\quad[0,1]\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\\
({f}^{y},{f}^{y}_{t})|_{t=0}=(f_{0}^{y},f^{y}_{1}),\end{cases}

(4.55)

where

\begin{split}&f_{0}^{y}=\chi(x-y)(\widetilde{f}_{0}-\widetilde{f}_{0}(y)),\quad f^{y}_{1}=\chi(x-y)\widetilde{f}_{1},\\
&\widetilde{f}_{0}=f_{0}(Tx),\quad\widetilde{f}_{1}=f_{1}(Tx).\end{split}

Let

\widetilde{f}=\textstyle{\sum}_{y\in 3^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\psi(x-y)({f}^{y}+\widetilde{f}_{0}(y)).

(4.56)

Seeing (
4.55
), using Proposition
4.2
again, for
k<r-1
, we shall obtain that

\begin{split}\|\left<\nabla\right>^{k-1}d{f}^{y}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,1]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq&C(\|\chi(x-y)(\widetilde{f}_{0}-\widetilde{f}_{0}(y))\|_{H_{x}^{r}}+\|\chi(x-y)\widetilde{f}_{1}\|_{H_{x}^{r-1}})\\
\leq&C(\|\widetilde{f}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{r}}+\|\widetilde{f}_{1}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{r-1}}).\end{split}

(4.57)

We again use the finite speed of propagation to conclude that
\widetilde{f}={f}^{y}+\widetilde{f}_{0}(y)
in
\mathrm{K}^{y}
. Using (
4.56
) and (
4.57
), so we get

\begin{split}\|\left<\nabla\right>^{k-1}d\widetilde{f}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,1]}L^{\infty}_{x}}=&\sup_{y\in 3^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\|\left<\nabla\right>^{k-1}d({f}^{y}+\widetilde{f}_{0}(y))\|_{L^{2}_{[0,1]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
=&\sup_{y\in 3^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\|\left<\nabla\right>^{k-1}d{f}^{y}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,1]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\\
\leq&C(\|\widetilde{f}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{r}}+\|\widetilde{f}_{1}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{r-1}}).\end{split}

(4.58)

By changing of coordinates from
(t,x)\rightarrow(T^{-1}t,T^{-1}x)
, we have

\begin{split}\|\left<\nabla\right>^{k-1}d{f}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,T]}L^{\infty}_{x}}=&T^{k-\frac{1}{2}}\|\left<\nabla\right>^{k-1}d\widetilde{f}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,1]}L^{\infty}_{x}}.\end{split}

(4.59)

Taking
T\leq 1
, combing (
4.58
) with (
4.59
), if
k<r-1
, then we have

\begin{split}\|\left<\nabla\right>^{k-1}d{f}\|_{L^{2}_{[0,T]}L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq&CT^{r-1-k}(\|{f}_{0}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{r}}+\|{f}_{1}\|_{\dot{H}_{x}^{r-1}})\\
\leq&C(\|{f}_{0}\|_{{H}_{x}^{r}}+\|{f}_{1}\|_{{H}_{x}^{r-1}}).\end{split}

(4.60)

By energy estimates for (
4.54
) and (
4.51
), we can carry out

\begin{split}\|d{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T]}H^{r-1}_{x}}\leq&C(\|{f}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{r}}+\|{f}_{1}\|_{H_{x}^{r-1}})\exp\{\|dg\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T]L^{\infty}_{x}}}\}\\
\leq&C_{M_{0}}(\|{f}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{r}}+\|{f}_{1}\|_{H_{x}^{r-1}})\exp\{\|d\mathbf{u},dh\|_{L^{1}_{[0,T]L^{\infty}_{x}}}\}\\
\leq&C_{M_{0}}(\|{f}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{r}}+\|{f}_{1}\|_{H_{x}^{r-1}}).\end{split}

(4.61)

By using
f=\int^{t}_{0}\partial_{t}fd\tau-f_{0}
, then

\begin{split}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T]}L^{2}_{x}}\leq&T\|d{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T]}L^{2}_{x}}+\|{f}_{0}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\\
\leq&C_{M_{0}}(\|{f}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{r}}+\|{f}_{1}\|_{H_{x}^{r-1}}).\end{split}

(4.62)

Adding (
4.61
) and (
4.62
), we therefore obtain

\begin{split}\|{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T]}H^{r}_{x}}+\|\partial_{t}{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[0,T]}H^{r-1}_{x}}\leq C_{M_{0}}(\|{f}_{0}\|_{H_{x}^{r}}+\|{f}_{1}\|_{H_{x}^{r-1}}).\end{split}

(4.63)

Because of (
4.60
)-(
4.63
), (
4.4
) and (
4.5
) hold. Thus, we give a complete proof for Proposition
4.1
.
∎

4.3.
Reduction to a bootstrap argument: Proposition
4.3

We will establish Proposition
4.2
via a continuity argument. For technical reasons, it is convenient for us to replace the original acoustic metric
g
by a truncated one. So we first introduce the process of truncation.

Set two vectors

\mathbf{0}=(0,0,0,0)^{\mathrm{T}},\quad\mathbf{e}=(1,0,0,0)^{\mathrm{T}}.

(4.64)

Then
h=0,\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{e},\mathbf{w}=\mathbf{0}
is a equilibrium state for System (
1.20
). So the data in (
4.14
) can be seen as a small perturbation around the equilibrium state. Taking
\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{e},h=0
in
g
, we record it
g(0)
. The inverse matrix of the metric
g(0)
is

g^{-1}(0)=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}-1&0&0&0\\
0&c^{2}_{s}(0)&0&0\\
0&0&c^{2}_{s}(0)&0\\
0&0&0&c^{2}_{s}(0)\end{array}\right).

By a linear change of coordinates which preserves
dt
, we may assume that
g^{\alpha\beta}(0)=m^{\alpha\beta}
. Let
\chi
be a smooth cut-off function supported in the region
B(0,3+2c)\times[-\frac{3}{2},\frac{3}{2}]
, which equals to
