1
in the region
B(0,2+2c)\times[-1,1]
. Let
\mathbf{g}=(\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta})_{4\times 4}
. Define

\begin{split}\mathbf{g}^{\alpha\beta}=&\chi(t,x)(g^{\alpha\beta}-g^{\alpha\beta}(0))+g^{\alpha\beta}(0),\end{split}

(4.65)

where
g^{\alpha\beta}=\Omega\{c_{s}^{2}m^{\alpha\beta}+(c_{s}^{2}-1)u^{\alpha}u^{\beta}\}
has been given by (
1.16
). Consider a wave-transport system

\begin{cases}\square_{\mathbf{g}}h=D,\\
\square_{\mathbf{g}}u^{\alpha}=-c^{2}_{s}\Omega\mathrm{e}^{-h}W^{\alpha}+Q^{\alpha},\\
u^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}w^{\alpha}=-u^{\alpha}w^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}h+w^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}u^{\alpha}-w^{\alpha}\partial_{\kappa}u^{\kappa},\\
\partial_{\alpha}w^{\alpha}=-w^{\kappa}\partial_{\kappa}h,\end{cases}

(4.66)

where
D
,
\Omega
,
W^{\alpha}
, and
Q^{\alpha}
have the formulations in (
1.21
), (
1.17
), (
1.18
) and (
1.22
).

We denote by
\mathcal{H}
the family of smooth solutions
(h,\mathbf{u},\mathbf{w})
to Equation (
4.66
) for
t\in[-2,2]
, with the initial data
(h_{0},\mathring{\mathbf{u}}_{0},\mathbf{w}_{0})
supported in
B(0,2+c)
, and for which

\|\mathring{\mathbf{u}}_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|h_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}}}\leq\epsilon_{3},

(4.67)

\|(\mathring{\mathbf{u}},u^{0}-1)\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s}}+\|h\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s_{0}}}\leq 2\epsilon_{2},

(4.68)

\|d\mathbf{u},dh\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}C_{x}^{\delta}}+\|dh,\nabla\mathbf{u}_{+},d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\leq 2\epsilon_{2}.

(4.69)

Then our bootstrap argument can be
stated as follows:

Proposition 4.3
.

Assume that (
1.36
) holds. Then there is a continuous functional
14
14

14

We set aside for the moment the definition of

\Re
.

\Re:\mathcal{H}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+}
, satisfying
\Re(0,\mathbf{e},\mathbf{0})=0
, so that for each
(h,\mathbf{u},\mathbf{w})\in\mathcal{H}
satisfying
\Re(h,\mathbf{u},\mathbf{w})\leq 2\epsilon_{1}
the following hold:

\mathrm{(1)}
The function
h
,
\mathbf{u}
, and
\mathbf{w}
satisfies
\Re(h,\mathbf{u})\leq\epsilon_{1}
.

\mathrm{(2)}
The following estimate holds,

\|(\mathring{\mathbf{u}},u^{0}-1)\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s}}+\|h\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s_{0}}}\leq\epsilon_{2},

(4.70)

\|d\mathbf{u},dh\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}C^{\delta}_{x}}+\|\nabla\mathbf{u}_{+},dh,d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\leq\epsilon_{2}.

(4.71)

\mathrm{(3)}
For any
1\leq k\leq s+1
, and for each
t_{0}\in[-2,2)
, the linear wave equation

\begin{cases}&\square_{\mathbf{g}}f=0,\qquad(t,x)\in(t_{0},2]\times\mathbb{R}^{3},\\
&(f,\partial_{t}f)|_{t=t_{0}}=(f_{0},f_{1})\in H^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\times H^{r-1}(\mathbb{R}^{3}),\end{cases}

(4.72)

admits a unique solution
f\in C([-2,2],H_{x}^{r})\times C^{1}([-2,2],H_{x}^{r-1})
. Furthermore, the Strichartz estimates (
4.18
) hold.

Based on Proposition
4.3
, we can prove
Proposition
4.2
as follows.

Proof of Proposition
4.2
by using Proposition
4.3
.

Consider the initial data in Proposition
4.2
satisfying

\|\mathring{\mathbf{u}}_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|h_{0}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\mathbf{w}_{0}\|_{H^{s_{0}}}\leq\epsilon_{3}.

Denote by
A
the subset of those
\gamma\in[0,1]
such that the equation (
4.66
) admits a smooth solution
(h_{\gamma},\mathbf{u}_{\gamma},\mathbf{w}_{\gamma})
having the initial data

\begin{split}h_{\gamma}(0)=&\gamma h_{0},\\
\mathring{\mathbf{u}}_{\gamma}(0)=&\gamma\mathring{\mathbf{u}}_{0},\\
u^{0}_{\gamma}(0)=&\sqrt{1+|\mathring{\mathbf{u}}_{\gamma}(0)|^{2}},\\
\mathbf{w}_{\gamma}(0)=&\mathrm{vort}(\mathrm{e}^{h_{\gamma}(0)}\mathbf{u}_{\gamma}(0)),\end{split}

and such that
\Re(h_{\gamma},\mathbf{u}_{\gamma},\mathbf{w}_{\gamma})\leq\epsilon_{1}
and (
4.70
), (
4.71
) hold. If
\gamma=0
, then

(h_{\gamma},{\mathbf{u}}_{\gamma},\mathbf{w}_{\gamma})(t,x)=(0,\mathbf{e},\mathbf{0}),

is a smooth solution of (
4.66
) with initial data

(h_{\gamma},{\mathbf{u}}_{\gamma},\mathbf{w}_{\gamma})(0,x)=(0,\mathbf{e},\mathbf{0}).

Thus, the set
A
is not empty. If we can prove that
\text{A}=[0,1]
, then
1\in\text{A}
. Also, (
4.14
)-(
4.18
) follow from Proposition
4.3
. As a result, Proposition
4.2
holds. It is sufficient to prove that
A
is both open and closed in
[0,1]
.

(1)
A
is open. Let
\gamma\in\text{A}
. Then
(h_{\gamma},\mathbf{u}_{\gamma},\mathbf{w}_{\gamma})
is a smooth solution to (
4.66
), where

\mathbf{w}_{\gamma}=\mathrm{vort}(\mathrm{e}^{h_{\gamma}}\mathbf{u}_{\gamma}).

Let
\beta
be close to
\gamma
. Since
(h^{\gamma},\mathbf{u}^{\gamma},\mathbf{w}^{\gamma})
is smooth, a perturbation argument
shows that the equation (
4.66
) has a smooth solution
(h^{\beta},\mathbf{u}^{\beta},\mathbf{w}^{\beta})
, which
depends continuously on
\beta
. By the continuity of
\Re
, for
\beta
close to
\gamma
, we infer

\Re(h_{\beta},\mathbf{u}_{\beta},\mathbf{w}_{\beta})\leq 2\epsilon_{1},

and (
4.67
), (
4.68
), (
4.69
) hold. Due to Proposition
4.3
, we get

\Re(h_{\beta},\mathbf{u}_{\beta},\mathbf{w}_{\beta})\leq\epsilon_{1},

and (
4.70
)-(
4.71
). Thus, we have showed that
\beta\in\text{A}
.

(2)
A
is closed. Let
\gamma_{k}\in\text{A},k\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
. Let
\gamma
be a limit satisfying
\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\gamma_{k}=\gamma
.
Then there exists a sequence
\{(h_{\gamma_{k}},\mathbf{u}_{\gamma_{k}},\mathbf{w}_{\gamma_{k}})\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{+}}
being the smooth solutions to (
4.66
) and

\displaystyle\|(u^{0}_{\gamma_{k}}-1,\mathring{\mathbf{u}}_{\gamma_{k}},h_{\gamma_{k}})\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s}}+\|(\partial_{t}\mathbf{u}_{\gamma_{k}},\partial_{t}h_{\gamma_{k}})\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s-1}}++\|\mathbf{w}_{\gamma_{k}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s_{0}}}

\displaystyle+\|\partial_{t}\mathbf{w}_{\gamma_{k}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s_{0}-1}}+\|d\mathbf{u}_{\gamma_{k}},dh_{\gamma_{k}}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}C_{x}^{\delta}}+\|d\mathbf{u}_{\gamma_{k}},dh_{\gamma_{k}}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}\dot{B}_{\infty,2}^{s_{0}-2}}\leq\epsilon_{2}.

Then there exists a subsequence
\{(h_{\gamma_{k}},\mathbf{u}_{\gamma_{k}},\mathbf{w}_{\gamma_{k}})\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}^{+}}
such that

\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}(h_{\gamma_{k}},\mathbf{u}_{\gamma_{k}},\mathbf{w}_{\gamma_{k}})=(h_{\gamma},\mathbf{u}_{\gamma},\mathbf{w}_{\gamma}).

Moreover,
(h_{\gamma},\mathbf{u}_{\gamma},\mathbf{w}_{\gamma})
satisfies

\begin{split}&\|(u^{0}_{\gamma}-1,\mathring{\mathbf{u}}_{\gamma},h_{\gamma})\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s}}+\|(\partial_{t}\mathbf{u}_{\gamma},\partial_{t}h_{\gamma})\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s-1}}+\|\mathbf{w}_{\gamma}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s_{0}}}\\
&\ +\|\partial_{t}\mathbf{w}_{\gamma}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s_{0}-1}}+\|d\mathbf{u}_{\gamma},dh_{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}C_{x}^{\delta}}+\|d\mathbf{u}_{\gamma},dh_{\gamma}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}\dot{B}_{\infty,2}^{s_{0}-2}}\leq\epsilon_{2},\end{split}

Thus,
\Re(h_{\gamma},\mathbf{u}_{\gamma},\mathbf{w}_{\gamma})\leq\epsilon_{1}
. Then
\gamma\in\text{A}
. At this stage, we have proved Proposition
4.2
.
∎

5.
Proof of Proposition
4.3

In this part, our goal is to prove Proposition
4.3
. Proposition
4.3
will follow as a result of Proposition
5.9
, Proposition
5.13
, and Proposition
5.17
. For a start, let us introduce a foliation of space-time and a definition of functional
\Re
.

5.1.
Geometric setup

Let the metric
\mathbf{g}
which equals the Minkowski metric for
t\in[-2,-\frac{3}{2}]
. Following Smith-Tataru’s paper
[
50
]
, for every
\theta\in\mathbb{S}^{2}
, we get a foliation of the slice
t=-2
by taking level sets of the function
r_{\theta}(-2,x)=\theta\cdot x+2
. Noting the fact that

\theta\cdot dx-dt
would then be a covector field over
t=-2
that is conormal to the level sets of
r_{\theta}(-2,x)
, so we set
\Gamma_{\theta}
to be the flowout of this section under the
Hamilton flow of
\mathbf{g}
. A key idea in the proof of Strichartz estimates is to establish that, for each
\theta
,
\Gamma_{\theta}
is the graph of a null covector field given by
dr_{\theta}
, where
r_{\theta}
is a smooth extension of
\theta\cdot x-t
, and that the level sets of
r_{\theta}
are small perturbations of the level sets of the function
\theta\cdot x-t
in a certain norm captured by
\Re
. In establishing Proposition
4.3
, we establish that
(h,\mathbf{u},\mathbf{w})\in\mathcal{H}
implies
\Gamma_{\theta}
is the graph of an appropriate null
covector field
dr_{\theta}
, so we only define
\Re(h,\mathbf{u},\mathbf{w})
in the situation. For the definition of
\Re
, see (
5.4
) below.

Assume that
\Gamma_{\theta}
and
r_{\theta}
are as above. Let
\Sigma_{\theta,r}
for
t\in\mathbb{R}
denote
the level sets of
r_{\theta}
. Thus, the characteristic hypersurface
\Sigma_{\theta,r}
is the flowout of
the set
\theta\cdot x=r-2
along the null geodesic flow in the direction
\theta
at
t=-2
.

Let us introduce an orthonormal sets of coordinates on
\mathbb{R}^{3}
by setting
x_{\theta}=\theta\cdot x
. Let
x^{\prime}_{\theta}
be given orthonormal coordinates on the hyperplane prependicular to
\theta
, which then define coordinate on
\mathbb{R}^{3}
by projection along
\theta
. Then
(t,x^{\prime}_{\theta})
induce the coordinates on
\Sigma_{\theta,r}
, and
\Sigma_{\theta,r}
is given by

\Sigma_{\theta,r}=\left\{(t,x):x_{\theta}-\phi_{\theta,r}=0\right\},

(5.1)

for a smooth function
\phi_{\theta,r}(t,x^{\prime}_{\theta})
. We now introduce two norms for functions defined on
[-2,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
,

\begin{split}&|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|f|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},\infty}=\sup_{-2\leq t\leq 2}\sup_{0\leq j\leq 1}\|\partial_{t}^{j}f(t,\cdot)\|_{H_{x}^{s_{0}-j}(\mathbb{R}^{3})},\\
&|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|f|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2}=\big(\sup_{0\leq j\leq 1}\int^{2}_{-2}\|\partial_{t}^{j}f(t,\cdot)\|^{2}_{H_{x}^{s_{0}-j}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}dt\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}.\end{split}

(5.2)

The same notation applies for functions in
[-2,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
. We denote

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|f|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma_{\theta,r}}=|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|f|_{\Sigma_{\theta,r}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2},

where the right hand side is the norm of the restriction of
f
to
{\Sigma_{\theta,r}}
, taken over the
(t,x^{\prime}_{\theta})
variables used to parametrise
{\Sigma_{\theta,r}}
. Similarly, the notation
\|f\|_{H^{s_{0}}(\Sigma_{\theta,r})}
denotes the
H^{s_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
norm of
f
restricted to the time
t
slice of
{\Sigma_{\theta,r}}
using the
x^{\prime}_{\theta}
coordinates on
{\Sigma^{t}_{\theta,r}}
. To avoid confusion, let
\Delta_{x^{\prime}}
be the standard Laplacian operator on
\Sigma
. We also define

\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}=(-\Delta_{x^{\prime}})^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(5.3)

We now define

\Re(h,\mathbf{u},\mathbf{w})=\sup_{\theta,r}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi_{\theta,r}-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,{\Sigma_{\theta,r}}}.

(5.4)

Due to (
5.4
), it suffices to consider
\theta=(0,0,1)
and
r=0
. We fix the choice, and suppress
\theta
and
r
in our notation. We use
(x_{3},x^{\prime})
instead of
(x_{\theta},x^{\prime}_{\theta})
. Then
\Sigma
is defined by

\Sigma=\left\{x_{3}-\phi(t,x^{\prime})=0\right\}.

The hypothesis
\Re(h,\mathbf{u},\mathbf{w})\leq 2\epsilon_{1}
(see Proposition
4.3
) implies that

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi_{\theta,r}(t,\cdot)-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}\leq 2\epsilon_{1}.

(5.5)

By Sobolev imbeddings, we have

\|d\phi(t,x^{\prime})-dt\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}C^{1,\delta_{0}}_{x^{\prime}}}+\|\partial_{t}d\phi(t,x^{\prime})\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}C^{\delta_{0}}_{x^{\prime}}}\lesssim\epsilon_{1}.

(5.6)

In the following, we will discuss energy estimates along characteristic hypersurfaces.

5.2.
Energy estimates along characteristic hypersurfaces

In this part, our goal is to establish energy estimates for
h,\mathbf{u}
and
\mathbf{w}
along the characteristic hypersurface
\Sigma
. We first introduce several inequalities on
\Sigma
as follows.

5.2.1.
Useful lemmas

Lemma 5.1

(
[
50
]
, Lemma 5.5)
.

Let
\tilde{f}(t,x)=f(t,x^{\prime},x_{3}+\phi(t,x^{\prime}))
. Then we have

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\tilde{f}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},\infty}\lesssim|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|f|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},\infty},\quad\|d\tilde{f}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}L_{x}^{\infty}}\lesssim\|df\|_{{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}L_{x}^{\infty}}},\quad\|\tilde{f}\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}\lesssim\|f\|_{H^{s_{0}}_{x}}.

Lemma 5.2

(
[
50
]
, Lemma 5.4)
.

For
r\geq 1
, we have

\begin{split}\sup_{t\in[-2,2]}\|f\|_{H^{r-\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}&\lesssim|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|f|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{r,2},\\
\sup_{t\in[-2,2]}\|f\|_{H^{r-\frac{1}{2}}(\Sigma^{t})}&\lesssim|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|f|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{r,2,\Sigma}.\end{split}

If
r>\frac{n+1}{2}
, then

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|f_{1}f_{2}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{r,2}\lesssim|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|f_{1}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{r,2}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|f_{2}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{r,2}.

Similarly, if
r>\frac{n}{2}
, then

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|f_{1}f_{2}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{r,2,\Sigma}\lesssim|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|f_{1}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{r,2,\Sigma}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|f_{2}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{r,2,\Sigma}.

Lemma 5.3

(
[
8
]
, Lemma 6.5)
.

Assume
2<s_{0}<s<\frac{5}{2}
. Let
\mathbf{U}=(p(h),{u}^{1},{u}^{2},{u}^{3})^{\mathrm{T}}
satisfy the symmetric hyperbolic system

A^{\alpha}(\mathbf{U})\partial_{\alpha}\mathbf{U}=0,

where
A^{\alpha}
(
\alpha=0,1,2,3
) is defined in (
2.13
)-(
2.16
).
Then we have the characteristic energy estimates

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathbf{U}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}&\lesssim\|d\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}L^{\infty}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s_{0}}}.\end{split}

5.2.2.
Characteristic energy estimates for enthalpy and velocity

For
(h,\mathbf{u},\mathbf{w})\in\mathcal{H}
, using (
4.68
) and (
4.69
), so we have

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|h,\mathring{\mathbf{u}},u^{0}-1|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s,\infty}+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathbf{w}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},\infty}+\|d\mathbf{u},dh,\nabla\mathbf{u}_{+}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}C^{\delta}_{x}}+\|dh,\nabla\mathbf{u}_{+},d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

(5.7)

Corollary 5.4
.

Suppose that
(h,\mathbf{u},\mathbf{w})\in\mathcal{H}
. Then we have

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|(u^{0}-1,\mathring{\mathbf{u}})|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|h|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

(5.8)

Proof.

By (
5.7
) and Lemma
5.3
, we obtain (
5.8
).
∎

Lemma 5.5
.

Let
\mathbf{U}=(p(h),u^{1},u^{2},u^{3})^{\mathrm{T}}
satisfy (
2.12
). Let
P_{\leq j}=\sum_{k=0}^{j}P_{k}
. Then

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|2^{j}(\mathbf{U}-P_{\leq j}\mathbf{U}),dP_{\leq j}\mathbf{U},2^{-j}d\nabla P_{\leq j}\mathbf{U}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-1,2,\Sigma}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

Proof.

Let
\Delta_{0}
be a standard multiplier of order
0
on
\mathbb{R}^{3}
, such that
\Delta_{0}
is additionally bounded on
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
. Clearly,

A^{0}(\mathbf{U})(\Delta_{0}U)_{t}+\sum^{3}_{i=1}A^{i}(\mathbf{U})(\Delta_{0}\mathbf{U})_{x_{i}}=-[\Delta_{0},A^{\alpha}(\mathbf{U})]\partial_{x_{\alpha}}\mathbf{U}.

Due to Lemma
5.3
, we have

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\Delta_{0}U|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|^{2}_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}\lesssim\|d\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\Delta_{0}\mathbf{U}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s}}+\|[\Delta_{0},A^{\alpha}(\mathbf{U})]\partial_{x_{\alpha}}\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}}}\|\Delta_{0}\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}}}.

(5.9)

By commutator estimates, we obtain

\|[\Delta_{0},A^{\alpha}(\mathbf{U})]\partial_{x_{\alpha}}\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}}}\lesssim\|d\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\Delta_{0}\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}}}.

Hence, (
5.9
) yields

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\Delta_{0}\mathbf{U}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|^{2}_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}\lesssim\|d\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\Delta_{0}\mathbf{U}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}}}.

(5.10)

To control the norm of
2^{j}(\mathbf{U}-P_{\leq j}\mathbf{U})
, we write

2^{j}(\mathbf{U}-P_{\leq j}\mathbf{U})=2^{j}\sum_{m\geq j}\Delta_{m}\mathbf{U},

where
\Delta_{m}\mathbf{U}
satisfies the above conditions for
\Delta_{0}\mathbf{U}
. Applying (
5.10
) and replacing
s_{0}
to
s_{0}-1
, we get

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|2^{j}(\mathbf{U}-P_{\leq j}\mathbf{U})|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|^{2}_{s_{0}-1,2,\Sigma}\lesssim&\sum_{m\geq j}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|2^{m}\Delta_{m}\mathbf{U}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|^{2}_{s_{0}-1,2,\Sigma}\\
\lesssim&\|d\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\sum_{m\geq j}(2^{m}\|\Delta_{m}\mathbf{U}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}}})\\
\lesssim&\|d\mathbf{U}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\mathbf{U}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s_{0}}}\lesssim\epsilon^{2}_{2}.\end{split}

Taking square of it, we can see

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|2^{j}(\mathbf{U}-P_{\leq j}\mathbf{U})|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-1,2,\Sigma}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

Finally, applying (
5.9
) to
\Delta_{0}=P_{\leq j}
and
\Delta_{0}=2^{-j}\nabla P_{\leq j}
shows that

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|dP_{\leq j}\mathbf{U}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-1,2,\Sigma}+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|2^{-j}d\nabla P_{\leq j}\mathbf{U}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-1,2,\Sigma}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

Therefore, the proof of Lemma
5.5
is completed.
∎

Based on the above lemmas, we are ready to prove the following result.

Proposition 5.6
.

Let
(h,\mathbf{u},\mathbf{w})\in\mathcal{H}
so that
\Re(h,\mathbf{u},\mathbf{w})\leq 2\epsilon_{1}
. Then

|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|{\mathbf{g}}^{\alpha\beta}-m^{\alpha\beta}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,{\Sigma_{\theta,r}}}+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|2^{j}({\mathbf{g}}^{\alpha\beta}-S_{j}{\mathbf{g}}^{\alpha\beta}),dS_{j}{\mathbf{g}}^{\alpha\beta},2^{-j}\nabla S_{j}d{\mathbf{g}}^{\alpha\beta}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-1,2,{\Sigma_{\theta,r}}}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

(5.11)

Proof.

Note
(h,\mathbf{u},\mathbf{w})\in\mathcal{H}
. By Lemma
5.5
, it is sufficient for us to verify that

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|{\mathbf{g}}^{\alpha\beta}-\mathbf{m}^{\alpha\beta}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma_{\theta,r}}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.\end{split}

By Lemma
5.3
, (
5.6
), and (
5.7
), we have

\sup_{\theta,r}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|(u^{0}-1,\mathring{\mathbf{u}})|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma_{\theta,r}}+\sup_{\theta,r}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|h|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma_{\theta,r}}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

Noting (
4.65
), and
\mathbf{g}^{00}=-1
, by Lemma
5.2
, and Corollary
5.4
we derive that

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|{\mathbf{g}}^{\alpha\beta}-\mathbf{m}^{\alpha\beta}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma_{\theta,r}}&\lesssim|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathbf{u}\cdot\mathring{\mathbf{u}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma_{\theta,r}}+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|c_{s}^{2}(h)-c_{s}^{2}(0)|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma_{\theta,r}}\\
&\lesssim|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathring{\mathbf{u}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma_{\theta,r}}+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|(u^{0}-1,\mathring{\mathbf{u}})\cdot\mathring{\mathbf{u}}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma_{\theta,r}}+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|h|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma_{\theta,r}}\\
&\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.\end{split}

Therefore, we have proved Proposition
5.6
.
∎

5.2.3.
Characteristic energy estimates for vorticity

We first introduce a lemma for transport equations.

Lemma 5.7

(
[
8
]
, Lemma 6.7)
.

Suppose that
(h,\mathbf{u},\mathbf{w})\in\mathcal{H}
. Let
f
satisfy the following transport equation

\mathbf{T}f=F,

(5.12)

where
\mathbf{T}
is defined in (
1.32
). Set
\mathrm{L}=\nabla(-\Delta)^{-1}\mathrm{curl}
. Then the following estimate holds

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathrm{L}f|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|^{2}_{s_{0}-2,2,\Sigma}\lesssim&\ \big(\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}\big)\|f\|^{2}_{{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}(1+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma})\\
&+\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}\left|\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda^{\sigma}_{x^{\prime}}{\mathrm{L}F}\cdot\Lambda^{\sigma}_{x^{\prime}}\mathrm{L}fdxd\tau\right|.\end{split}

(5.13)

Here,
\Lambda_{x^{\prime}}
is defined in (
5.3
).

We next give the characteristic energy estimates for vorticity as follows.

Lemma 5.8
.

Suppose that
(h,\mathbf{u},\mathbf{w})\in\mathcal{H}
. Let
\mathbf{W}
be defined in (
1.18
). Then

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathbf{W}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-1,2,\Sigma}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.\end{split}

(5.14)

Proof.

Due to (
5.2
), we have

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathbf{W}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-1,2,\Sigma}=&\|\mathbf{W}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}}+\|\partial_{t}\mathbf{W}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}}\\
=&\|\mathbf{W}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\|\Lambda^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}\mathbf{W}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\|\partial_{t}\mathbf{W}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}}.\end{split}

(5.15)

The bound for
\|\mathbf{W}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}
. For (
2.15
), by changing of coordinates
x_{3}\rightarrow x_{3}-\phi(t,x^{\prime})
, multiplying
\mathbf{W}
and integrating (
2.17
) on
[-2,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we infer

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{W}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\lesssim&\ \|\nabla\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}(\|\nabla\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{W}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}})\|\mathbf{W}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}.\end{split}

(5.16)

Taking square of (
5.16
), and using (
4.15
)-(
4.69
), we then derive

\|\mathbf{W}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.

(5.17)

The bound for
\|\Lambda^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}\mathbf{W}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}
. To bound
\|\Lambda^{s_{0}-1}_{x^{\prime}}\mathbf{W}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}
, so consider
\|\nabla\mathbf{\mathring{\mathbf{W}}}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}
and
\|\nabla W^{0}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}
. By Hodge decomposition, we have

\begin{split}\|\nabla\mathbf{\mathring{\mathbf{W}}}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}&=\|\mathrm{L}(\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{\mathring{\mathbf{W}}})+\mathrm{H}(\mathrm{div}\mathbf{\mathring{\mathbf{W}}})\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\\
&\leq\|\mathrm{L}(\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{\mathring{\mathbf{W}}})\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\|\mathrm{H}(\mathrm{div}\mathbf{\mathring{\mathbf{W}}})\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)},\end{split}

(5.18)

where the operators
\mathrm{L}
and
\mathrm{H}
are given by

\mathrm{L}=\nabla(-\Delta)^{-1}\mathrm{curl},\quad\mathrm{H}=(-\Delta)^{-1}\nabla^{2}.

(5.19)

For
\mathrm{div}\mathbf{\mathring{\mathbf{W}}}=\partial_{i}W^{i}=\partial_{\alpha}W^{\alpha}-\partial_{t}W^{0}
, we can carry out

\begin{split}\|\mathrm{H}(\mathrm{div}\mathbf{\mathring{\mathbf{W}}})\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\leq&\|\mathrm{H}(\partial_{\alpha}W^{\alpha})\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\|\mathrm{H}(\partial_{t}W^{0})\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}.\end{split}

(5.20)

By (
2.4.2
), trace theorem, and Hölder’s inequality, it follows

\begin{split}\|\mathrm{H}(\partial_{\alpha}W^{\alpha})\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\lesssim&\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\cdot d\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\|\mathbf{w}\cdot d\mathbf{u}\cdot dh\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\\
\lesssim&\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\cdot d\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-3/2}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\cdot d\mathbf{u}\cdot dh\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-3/2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}(\|d\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}}_{x}}\|d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}).\end{split}

(5.21)

Due to (
2.4.2
) and (
2.112
), using trace theorem and Hölder’s inequality, we have

\begin{split}\|\mathrm{H}(\partial_{t}W^{0})\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\leq&\|\mathring{\mathbf{W}}\cdot d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\|(u^{0})^{-1}\mathring{\mathbf{u}}\cdot\partial_{t}\mathring{\mathbf{W}}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\\
\leq&C\|\mathring{\mathbf{W}}\cdot d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+|(u^{0})^{-1}\mathring{\mathbf{u}}|\cdot\|\partial_{t}\mathring{\mathbf{W}}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\\
\leq&|(u^{0})^{-1}\mathring{\mathbf{u}}|^{2}\cdot\|\nabla\mathring{\mathbf{W}}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+C\|{\mathbf{W}}\cdot(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\\
&+C\|d{\mathbf{w}}\cdot(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+C\|{\mathbf{w}}\cdot d\mathbf{u}\cdot dh\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\\
\leq&(\frac{|\mathring{\mathbf{u}}|}{|u^{0}|})^{2}\cdot\|\nabla\mathring{\mathbf{W}}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+C\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}\|d\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}\\
&+C\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}}_{x}}\|d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}.\end{split}

(5.22)

Gathering (
5.18
), (
5.20
), (
5.21
), and (
5.22
), we can prove

\begin{split}(1-\frac{|\mathring{\mathbf{u}}|^{2}}{|u^{0}|^{2}})\|\nabla\mathbf{\mathring{\mathbf{W}}}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\leq&\|\mathrm{L}(\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{\mathring{\mathbf{W}}})\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+C\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}\|d\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}\\
&+C\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}}_{x}}\|d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}.\end{split}

Note
1\leq|u^{0}|\leq 1+C_{0}
. By using (
1.1
), (
2.12
), (
2.17
), and (
5.7
), we therefore get

\begin{split}\|\nabla\mathbf{\mathring{\mathbf{W}}}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\lesssim&\|\mathrm{L}(\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{\mathring{\mathbf{W}}})\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}\|d\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}\\
&+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}}_{x}}\|d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\|\mathrm{L}(\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{\mathring{\mathbf{W}}})\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\epsilon_{2}^{2}.\end{split}

(5.23)

By (
2.109
), we can obtain

\begin{split}\|\nabla W^{0}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}&\lesssim\|\nabla\mathbf{\mathring{\mathbf{W}}}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\|\mathbf{W}d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}.\end{split}

(5.24)

By trace theorem, (
1.18
), and (
5.7
), we have

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{W}d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\lesssim&\|\mathbf{W}d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1/2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\|d\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}\|d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}}_{x}}\|d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}\|dh\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\epsilon^{2}_{2}.\end{split}

(5.25)

Combining (
5.23
), (
5.24
), and (
5.25
), we can prove that

\begin{split}\|\nabla\mathbf{{\mathbf{W}}}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\lesssim\|\mathrm{L}(\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{\mathring{\mathbf{W}}})\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\epsilon^{2}_{2}.\end{split}

(5.26)

In (
5.26
), we still need to bound
\|\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}\mathrm{L}(\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{\mathring{\mathbf{W}}})\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}
. By (
2.93
), we have

\begin{split}&\|\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}\mathrm{L}(\mathrm{curl}\mathbf{\mathring{\mathbf{W}}})\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\\
\lesssim&\|\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}\mathrm{L}(\mathbf{u}\cdot{\mathbf{G}})\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\|\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}\mathrm{L}(\mathbf{W}\cdot d\mathbf{u})\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\|\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}\mathrm{L}(\mathbf{w}\cdot d\mathbf{u}\cdot dh)\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\\
\lesssim&\|\mathbf{u}\cdot\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}\mathrm{L}\mathbf{G}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\|[\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}\mathrm{L},\mathbf{u}]\mathbf{G}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\|\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}\mathrm{L}(\mathbf{W}\cdot d\mathbf{u})\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\\
&+\|\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}\mathrm{L}(\mathbf{w}\cdot d\mathbf{u}\cdot dh)\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}.\end{split}

(5.27)

By Sobolev imbeddings, trace theorem and (
5.7
), we can obtain

\begin{split}\|[\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}\mathrm{L},\mathbf{u}]\mathbf{G}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\lesssim&\|\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}C_{x^{\prime}}^{s_{0}-2}(\Sigma)}\|\mathbf{G}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\\
\lesssim&(\|u^{0}-1,\mathring{\mathbf{u}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x^{\prime}}^{s_{0}-1/2}(\Sigma)}+1)\|\mathbf{G}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\\
\lesssim&(\|u^{0}-1,\mathring{\mathbf{u}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s}}+1)\|\mathbf{G}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\\
\lesssim&\|\mathbf{G}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}.\end{split}

(5.28)

Due to Hölder’s inequality, Sobolev imbeddings, trace theorem and (
5.7
), we have

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{u}\cdot\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}\mathrm{L}\mathbf{G}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\lesssim&\|\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\|\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}\mathrm{L}\mathbf{G}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\\
\lesssim&(\|u^{0}-1,\mathring{\mathbf{u}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H_{x}^{s}}+1)\|\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}\mathrm{L}\mathbf{G}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\\
\lesssim&\|\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}\mathrm{L}\mathbf{G}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}.\end{split}

(5.29)

Due to trace theorem and (
5.7
), we have

\begin{split}\|\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}\mathrm{L}\left(\mathbf{W}\cdot d\mathbf{u}\right)\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\lesssim&\|\mathrm{L}\left(\mathbf{W}\cdot d\mathbf{u}\right)\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s-3/2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\|\mathbf{W}\cdot d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-3/2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\|d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}\|\mathbf{W}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{1}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\epsilon^{2}_{2}.\end{split}

(5.30)

Similarly, we can obtain

\begin{split}\|\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}\mathrm{L}\left((\mathbf{w}\cdot d\mathbf{u}\cdot dh)\right)\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\lesssim&\|\mathrm{L}\left(\mathbf{w}\cdot d\mathbf{u}\cdot dh\right)\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s-3/2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\|\mathbf{w}\cdot d\mathbf{u}\cdot dh\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-3/2}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\|d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}\|dh\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\epsilon^{3}_{2}.\end{split}

(5.31)

To summarize from (
5.26
), (
5.27
), (
5.28
), (
5.29
), (
5.30
), and (
5.31
), we get

\begin{split}\|\nabla\mathbf{W}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\lesssim&\|\mathbf{G}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\|\Lambda^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}\mathrm{L}\mathbf{G}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}+\epsilon_{2}^{2}.\end{split}

(5.32)

The bound for
\|\mathbf{G}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}
.
By (
2.28
), we can see

\begin{split}\mathbf{T}\left(G^{\alpha}-F^{\alpha}\right)=&K^{\alpha},\end{split}

(5.33)

where

K^{\alpha}=(u^{0})^{-1}E^{\alpha}+\partial^{\alpha}\left((u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma\right)-\Gamma\partial^{\alpha}\left((u^{0})^{-1}\right).

(5.34)

By changing of coordinates
x_{3}\rightarrow x_{3}-\phi(t,x^{\prime})
, then (
5.33
) becomes to

\begin{split}(\partial_{t}+\partial_{t}\phi\partial_{3})(\widetilde{G}^{\alpha}-\widetilde{F}^{\alpha})+\frac{\widetilde{u}^{i}}{\widetilde{u}^{0}}\cdot(\partial_{i}+\partial_{i}\phi\partial_{3})(\widetilde{G}^{\alpha}-\widetilde{F}^{\alpha})=&\widetilde{K}^{\alpha},\end{split}

(5.35)

where

\begin{split}\widetilde{K}^{\alpha}=&(\partial^{\alpha}+\partial^{\alpha}\phi\partial_{3})\left((u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma\right)+(\widetilde{u}^{0})^{-1}\widetilde{E}^{\alpha}-\widetilde{\Gamma}(\partial^{\alpha}+\partial^{\alpha}\phi\partial_{3})\left((\widetilde{u}^{0})^{-1}\right).\end{split}

Multiplying
(\widetilde{G}_{\alpha}-\widetilde{F}_{\alpha})
on (
5.35
) and integrating it on
[-2,2]\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we therefore get

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathbf{G}-\mathbf{F}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|^{2}_{0,2,\Sigma}=\mathrm{J_{1}}+\mathrm{J_{2}}+\mathrm{J_{3}}+\mathrm{J_{4}}+\mathrm{J_{5}},\end{split}

(5.36)

where
\mathrm{J_{1}},\mathrm{J_{2}},\cdots,\mathrm{J_{5}}
are given by

\begin{split}\mathrm{J_{1}}=&-\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{t}\phi\partial_{3}(\widetilde{G}^{\alpha}-\widetilde{F}^{\alpha})\cdot(\widetilde{G}_{\alpha}-\widetilde{F}_{\alpha})dxd\tau,\\
\mathrm{J_{2}}=&-\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\frac{\widetilde{u}^{i}}{\widetilde{u}^{0}}\cdot(\partial_{i}+\partial_{i}\phi\partial_{3})(\widetilde{G}^{\alpha}-\widetilde{F}^{\alpha})\cdot(\widetilde{G}_{\alpha}-\widetilde{F}_{\alpha})dxd\tau,\\
\mathrm{J_{3}}=&\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\widetilde{u}^{0})^{-1}\widetilde{E}^{\alpha}\cdot(\widetilde{G}_{\alpha}-\widetilde{F}_{\alpha})dxd\tau,\\
\mathrm{J_{4}}=&\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\partial^{\alpha}+\partial^{\alpha}\phi\partial_{3})\{(\widetilde{u}^{0})^{-1}\widetilde{\Gamma}\}\cdot(\widetilde{G}_{\alpha}-\widetilde{F}_{\alpha})dxd\tau,\\
\mathrm{J_{5}}=&-\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\widetilde{\Gamma}(\partial^{\alpha}+\partial^{\alpha}\phi\partial_{3})\left((\widetilde{u}^{0})^{-1}\right)\cdot(\widetilde{G}_{\alpha}-\widetilde{F}_{\alpha})dxd\tau.\end{split}

For
\phi
is independent of
x_{3}
, if we integrate
\mathrm{J_{1}}
by parts, then we have

\mathrm{J_{1}}=0.

(5.37)

Integrating
\mathrm{J_{2}}
by parts, and using
\phi
independent of
x_{3}
, we can obtain

\begin{split}\mathrm{J_{2}}=&\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{i}(\frac{\widetilde{u}^{i}}{\widetilde{u}^{0}})(\widetilde{G}^{\alpha}-\widetilde{F}^{\alpha})\cdot(\widetilde{G}_{\alpha}-\widetilde{F}_{\alpha})dxd\tau\\
&+\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{i}\phi\partial_{3}(\frac{\widetilde{u}^{i}}{\widetilde{u}^{0}})(\widetilde{G}^{\alpha}-\widetilde{F}^{\alpha})\cdot(\widetilde{G}_{\alpha}-\widetilde{F}_{\alpha})dxd\tau.\end{split}

Using Hölder’s inequality, it follows

\begin{split}|\mathrm{J_{2}}|\lesssim&\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}(1+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma})\|\mathbf{G}-\mathbf{F}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}.\end{split}

(5.38)

Inserting (
3.2
), (
3.111
) to (
5.38
), and using (
5.7
), it yields

\begin{split}|\mathrm{J_{2}}|\lesssim&\|d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{2}_{x}})(1+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}})\\
&+\|d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{2}_{x}})\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{3}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}\\
&+(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{2}_{x}})(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}})\\
&+(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{2}_{x}})(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{3}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{4}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}})\\
\lesssim&\epsilon^{2}_{2}.\end{split}

(5.39)

By (
3.2
), (
3.110
), (
3.111
), (
5.7
) and using Hölder’s inequality again, we have

\begin{split}&|\mathrm{J_{3}}|+|\mathrm{J_{5}}|\\
\lesssim&\int^{2}_{-2}\|\mathbf{E}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|\mathbf{G}-\mathbf{F}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}d\tau+\int^{2}_{-2}\|\Gamma\cdot d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|\mathbf{G}-\mathbf{F}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}d\tau\\
\lesssim&\|d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{2}_{x}})(1+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}})\\
&+\|d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{2}_{x}})\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{3}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}\\
&+(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{2}_{x}})(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}})\\
&+(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{2}_{x}})(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{3}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{4}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}})\\
\lesssim&\epsilon^{2}_{2}.\end{split}

(5.40)

For
\mathrm{J_{4}}
, by changing of coordinates
x_{3}\rightarrow x_{3}+\phi(t,x^{\prime})
, we obtain

\begin{split}\mathrm{J_{4}}=&\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial^{\alpha}\{({u}^{0})^{-1}{\Gamma}\}\cdot({G}_{\alpha}-{F}_{\alpha})dxd\tau.\end{split}

By chain rule, we further calculate
\mathrm{J_{4}}
by

\begin{split}\mathrm{J_{4}}=&\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial^{\alpha}\{({u}^{0})^{-1}{\Gamma}\cdot{G}_{\alpha}\}dx-\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}({u}^{0})^{-1}{\Gamma}\cdot\partial^{\alpha}{G}_{\alpha}dxd\tau\\
&-\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial^{\alpha}\{({u}^{0})^{-1}{\Gamma}\cdot{F}_{\alpha}\}dx-\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}({u}^{0})^{-1}{\Gamma}\cdot\partial^{\alpha}{F}_{\alpha}dx\\
=&\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}({u}^{0})^{-1}\Gamma\cdot G^{0}dx\right)\big|^{2}_{-2}-\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}({u}^{0})^{-1}{\Gamma}\cdot\partial^{\alpha}{G}_{\alpha}dxd\tau\\
&-\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}({u}^{0})^{-1}\Gamma\cdot F^{0}dx\right)\big|^{2}_{-2}+\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}({u}^{0})^{-1}{\Gamma}\cdot\partial^{\alpha}{F}_{\alpha}dx.\end{split}

(5.41)

Inserting (
5.41
) to (
5.36
), it follows

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathbf{G}-\mathbf{F}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|^{2}_{0,2,\Sigma}=&\mathrm{J_{1}}+\mathrm{J_{2}}+\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}({u}^{0})^{-1}\Gamma\cdot G^{0}dx\right)\big|^{2}_{-2}-\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}({u}^{0})^{-1}\Gamma\cdot F^{0}dx\right)\big|^{2}_{-2}\\
&+\mathrm{J_{3}}+\mathrm{J_{5}}-\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}({u}^{0})^{-1}{\Gamma}\cdot\partial^{\alpha}{G}_{\alpha}dx+\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}({u}^{0})^{-1}{\Gamma}\cdot\partial^{\alpha}{F}_{\alpha}dx.\end{split}

(5.42)

Note
1\leq u^{0}\leq 1+C_{0}
. Referring (
3.115
)-(
3.118
), and using (
5.7
), we can derive that

\begin{split}&\left|\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}({u}^{0})^{-1}{\Gamma}\cdot\partial^{\alpha}{G}_{\alpha}dx\right|\\
\lesssim&\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{2}_{x}})\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}\\
&+(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{2}_{x}})\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{3}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\epsilon_{2}^{2}.\end{split}

(5.43)

In a similar way, by referring (
3.116
)-(
3.137
) and using (
5.7
), we can obtain

\begin{split}&\left|\int^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}({u}^{0})^{-1}{\Gamma}\cdot\partial^{\alpha}{F}_{\alpha}dx\right|\\
\lesssim&\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{2}_{x}})\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}\\
&+(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{2}_{x}})(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{3}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}})\\
\lesssim&\epsilon_{2}^{2}.\end{split}

(5.44)

By Hölder’s inequality, (
2.29
), (
3.111
), and (
5.7
), we also derive

\begin{split}&\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}({u}^{0})^{-1}\Gamma\cdot G^{0}dx\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}}\\
\lesssim&(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{2}_{x}})(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{3}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}})\\
\lesssim&\epsilon_{2}^{2},\end{split}

(5.45)

and

\begin{split}&\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}({u}^{0})^{-1}\Gamma\cdot F^{0}dx\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}}\\
\lesssim&(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{2}_{x}})(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{3}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s-1}_{x}})\\
\lesssim&\epsilon_{2}^{2}.\end{split}

(5.46)

To summarize (
5.42
), (
5.37
), (
5.39
), (
5.40
), (

), (
5.44
), (

), and (

), we can find that

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathbf{G}-\mathbf{F}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|^{2}_{0,2,\Sigma}\lesssim&\epsilon_{2}^{2}.\end{split}

(5.47)

The bound for
|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathrm{L}\mathbf{G}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-2,2,\Sigma}
.
By Lemma
5.7
and (
5.7
), we have

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathrm{L}\left(\mathbf{G}-\mathbf{F}\right)|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|^{2}_{s_{0}-2,2,\Sigma}\lesssim&\|\nabla\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}\dot{B}^{s_{0}-2}_{\infty,2}}\|\mathbf{G}-\mathbf{F}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}(1+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma})\\
&+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma}\|\mathbf{G}-\mathbf{F}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}{H}^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}(1+|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|d\phi-dt|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0},2,\Sigma})\\
&+\textstyle{\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}}\big|{\int}^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda^{\sigma}_{x^{\prime}}{\mathrm{L}K^{\alpha}}\cdot\Lambda^{\sigma}_{x^{\prime}}\mathrm{L}(G_{\alpha}-F_{\alpha})dxd\tau\big|\\
\lesssim&\epsilon_{2}^{2}+\underbrace{\textstyle{\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}}\big|{\int}^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda^{\sigma}_{x^{\prime}}{\mathrm{L}K^{\alpha}}\cdot\Lambda^{\sigma}_{x^{\prime}}\mathrm{L}(G_{\alpha}-F_{\alpha})dxd\tau\big|}_{\equiv\mathrm{J}_{6}}.\end{split}

(5.48)

From (
5.34
), we divide
\mathrm{J}_{6}
into

\begin{split}\mathrm{J}_{6}=&\underbrace{\textstyle{\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}}\big|{\int}^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda^{\sigma}_{x^{\prime}}{\mathrm{L}\left\{\partial^{\alpha}\left((u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma\right)\right\}}\cdot\Lambda^{\sigma}_{x^{\prime}}\mathrm{L}(G_{\alpha}-F_{\alpha})dxd\tau\big|}_{\equiv\mathrm{J}_{61}}\\
&+\underbrace{\textstyle{\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}}\big|{\int}^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda^{\sigma}_{x^{\prime}}{\mathrm{L}\left\{({u}^{0})^{-1}{E}^{\alpha}\right\}}\cdot\Lambda^{\sigma}_{x^{\prime}}\mathrm{L}(G_{\alpha}-F_{\alpha})dxd\tau\big|}_{\equiv\mathrm{J}_{62}}\\
&+\underbrace{\textstyle{\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}}\big|{\int}^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda^{\sigma}_{x^{\prime}}{\mathrm{L}\left\{{\Gamma}\partial^{\alpha}\left(({u}^{0})^{-1}\right)\right\}}\cdot\Lambda^{\sigma}_{x^{\prime}}\mathrm{L}(G_{\alpha}-F_{\alpha})dxd\tau\big|}_{\equiv\mathrm{J}_{63}}.\end{split}

(5.49)

To bound
\mathrm{J}_{6}
, we need to discuss
\mathrm{J}_{61}
,
\mathrm{J}_{62}
and
\mathrm{J}_{63}
as follows. By chain rule, we get

\begin{split}\mathrm{J}_{61}\leq&\underbrace{{\textstyle{\sum}_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}}\big|{\int}^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial^{\alpha}\left\{\Lambda^{\sigma}_{x^{\prime}}{\mathrm{L}\left((u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma\right)}\cdot\Lambda^{\sigma}_{x^{\prime}}\mathrm{L}(G_{\alpha}-F_{\alpha})\right\}dxd\tau\big|}_{\equiv\mathrm{J}_{61a}}\\
&+\underbrace{{\textstyle{\sum}_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}}\big|{\int}^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda^{\sigma}_{x^{\prime}}{\mathrm{L}\left((u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma\right)}\cdot\Lambda^{\sigma}_{x^{\prime}}\mathrm{L}\partial^{\alpha}G_{\alpha}dxd\tau\big|}_{\equiv\mathrm{J}_{61b}}\\
&+\underbrace{{\textstyle{\sum}_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}}\big|{\int}^{2}_{-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda^{\sigma}_{x^{\prime}}{\mathrm{L}\left((u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma\right)}\cdot\Lambda^{\sigma}_{x^{\prime}}\mathrm{L}\partial^{\alpha}F_{\alpha}dxd\tau\big|}_{\equiv\mathrm{J}_{61c}}.\end{split}

(5.50)

By a direct calculation, it follows

\begin{split}\mathrm{J}_{61a}=&{\textstyle{\sum}_{\sigma\in\{0,s_{0}-2\}}}\left|\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\Lambda^{\sigma}_{x^{\prime}}{\mathrm{L}\left((u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma\right)}\cdot\Lambda^{\sigma}_{x^{\prime}}\mathrm{L}(G^{0}-F^{0})dx\right)\big|^{2}_{-2}\right|\\
\lesssim&\|(u^{0})^{-1}\Gamma\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}(\|\mathbf{G}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{F}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x}})\\
\lesssim&(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}})\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}-\frac{1}{2}}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\epsilon^{2}_{2}.\end{split}

(5.51)

Above, we also use (
5.7
). While, for
\mathrm{J}_{61b}
, recalling the term
\mathrm{H}_{111}
in (
3.45
), so we find that
\mathrm{J}_{61b}
can be controlled in a same way for
\mathrm{H}_{111}
. Therefore, from (
3.48
), we have

\begin{split}\mathrm{J}_{61b}\lesssim&(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}_{\infty,2}^{s_{0}-2}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}})\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}\\
&+(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}_{\infty,2}^{s_{0}-2}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}})\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}}_{x}}\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}\\
&+(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}}_{x}})\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{3}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}.\end{split}

Due to (
5.7
), it yields

\begin{split}\mathrm{J}_{61b}\lesssim&\epsilon^{2}_{2}.\end{split}

(5.52)

For
\mathrm{J}_{61c}
, recalling the term
\mathrm{H}_{121}
in (
3.46
), so we find that
\mathrm{J}_{61c}
can be controlled in a same way for
\mathrm{H}_{121}
. Therefore, from (
3.70
), we have

\begin{split}\mathrm{J}_{61c}\lesssim&(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}_{\infty,2}^{s_{0}-2}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}})(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}}_{x}})\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}\\
&+(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}}_{x}})(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{3}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}).\end{split}

By (
5.7
), we have proved

\begin{split}\mathrm{J}_{61c}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}^{2}.\end{split}

(5.53)

Since (
5.50
), using (
5.51
), (
5.52
), together with (
5.53
), we have obtained

\begin{split}\mathrm{J}_{61}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}^{2}.\end{split}

(5.54)

For
\mathrm{J}_{62}
, using Hölder’s inequality and (
5.7
), we can show that

\begin{split}\mathrm{J}_{62}\lesssim&\|\mathbf{E}\|_{L^{1}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x}}\|\mathbf{G}-\mathbf{F}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-2}}\\
\lesssim&(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}_{\infty,2}^{s_{0}-2}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}})\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}}_{x}}\\
&+(\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{5}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}})\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\epsilon_{2}^{2}.\end{split}

(5.55)

For
\mathrm{J}_{63}
, using Hölder’s inequality and (
5.7
), we can also bound it by

\begin{split}\mathrm{J}_{63}\lesssim&\|\Gamma\cdot d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{1}_{t}{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-2}}\|\mathbf{G}-\mathbf{F}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}{H}_{x}^{s_{0}-2}}\\
\lesssim&(\|d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}_{\infty,2}^{s_{0}-2}}+\|d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}})\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}}_{x}}(1+\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}})\\
&+(\|d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{2}_{t}\dot{B}_{\infty,2}^{s_{0}-2}}+\|d\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}})\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}}_{x}}\|(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|^{3}_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{s_{0}-1}_{x}}\\
\lesssim&\epsilon_{2}^{2}.\end{split}

(5.56)

To summarize our outcome (
5.48
), (
5.49
), (
5.54
), (
5.55
), and (
5.56
), we can conclude that

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathrm{L}\left(\mathbf{G}-\mathbf{F}\right)|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|^{2}_{s_{0}-2,2,\Sigma}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}^{2}.\end{split}

(5.57)

By trace theorem, (
2.30
), Hölder’s inequality, and (
5.7
), we have

\begin{split}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathrm{L}\mathbf{F}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|_{s_{0}-2,2,\Sigma}\lesssim&\|\mathrm{L}\mathbf{F}\|_{L^{2}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s_{0}-\frac{3}{2}}}\\
\lesssim&\|d\mathbf{w}\cdot(d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s_{0}-\frac{3}{2}}}+\|\mathbf{w}\cdot dh\cdot dh\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s_{0}-\frac{3}{2}}}\\
\lesssim&\|d\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s_{0}-1}}\|d\mathbf{u},dh)\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{1}}+\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{s_{0}}}\|dh\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{[-2,2]}H_{x}^{1}}\\
\lesssim&\epsilon_{2}^{2}.\end{split}

(5.58)

Since (
5.57
), (
5.58
), and (
5.47
), so (
5.32
) becomes

\begin{split}\|\nabla\mathbf{W}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.\end{split}

(5.59)

The bound for
\|\partial_{t}\mathbf{W}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}
.
For
\partial_{x^{\prime}}\mathbf{W}=\nabla\mathbf{W}\cdot(0,\partial_{x^{\prime}}\phi)^{\mathrm{T}}
, using (

), it yields

\begin{split}\|\partial_{x^{\prime}}\mathbf{W}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.\end{split}

(5.60)

By using (

), (

) and (

), we infer

\begin{split}\|\partial_{t}\mathbf{W}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s_{0}-2}_{x^{\prime}}(\Sigma)}\lesssim\epsilon_{2}.\end{split}

(5.61)

Adding (

), (
